L1303: ## 5.2 Review Strategy
L1304: ## Efficacy review:
L1305: The review strategy will focus on the following questions:
L1306: - Is NT 201 (b) (4) doses of 120 units and 240 units) superior to placebo in the treatment of cervical dystonia?
- The review will rely primarily on study 408/1 for the evaluation of effectiveness. The primary source of efficacy data will be TWSTRS Total Score at Week 4 (Visit 3) compared to the TWSTRS Total Score at the Baseline Visit. This 20 week (12-to 16-weeks treatment period) duration pivotal study (408/1) presented by the Sponsor is a randomized, multicenter trial to investigate the efficacy and safety of the 120 and 240 unit doses of NT 201 in the treatment of cervical dystonia. This is the only study that included sites in the United States.
- The FDA agreed to accept data from a single pivotal trial in CD with supporting data coming from the Blepharospasm (another focal dystonia) pivotal trial. The Meeting Discussion from June 11, 2007 clarified this in the meeting minutes:*"The Division agreed that the currently ongoing CD trial in pretreated and treatment naïve patients combined with the currently ongoing Blepharospasm trial in pretreated patients appeared capable of fulfilling the requirement for two adequate and well controlled trials necessary for a BLA in CD".*
- The sponsor had also submitted efficacy data from Study MRZ 60201-0013 which supported the approval of IncobotulinumtoxinA for the CD indication in Europe. The
L1307: Anne E. A. Constantino, MD
L1308: NDA 125360-1
L1309: Botulinum Toxin A, Xeomin® IncobotulinumtoxinA
L1310: trial is designed as a non-inferiority trial comparing NT 201 to Botox. The trial will not be relied upon heavily because of its lack of assay sensitivity (there was no placebo arm) but will be analyzed for safety.
L1311: ## 2. Is there an added benefit to treating patients with 240 units instead of the 120 units?
L1312: The data from the pivotal study 408/1 and to a lesser degree the extension study 408/2 will be used to analyze data to evaluate the benefits of a 240 units over 120 units.
L1313: - What are the significant differences in the TWSTRS scores (if any) of those who had received 240 compared to 120 units of NT 201?
- Are there any subgroups that have benefitted from the 240 units more than 120 units of NT 201?
- What is the effect of duration (from previous dose) and frequency of injections in the dose response, if any, between the two treatment groups?
L1314: ## 3. What is the duration of effectiveness of NT 201?
L1315: - The data from the Extension Phase of trial (408/2) will provide data regarding duration of treatment effect and sustained benefit.
L1316: A separate efficacy review of NT 201 for the indication of Blepharospasm was assigned to Dr. Kenneth Bergman, MD, FAAN.
L1317: ## Safety Review:
L1318: This clinical reviewer looked at safety data review from all the clinical trials listed in the following:
L1319: - Pivotal trial 408/1
- Extension Phase of Clinical Trial 408/2
- Non inferiority Clinical Trial done in Europe: Clinical Trial 0013
L1320: The safety review was assigned to Dr. Lisa Jones, MD, MPH.
L1321: ## 5.3 Discussion of Individual Studies/Clinical Trials
L1322: ## Trial: Study Protocol: MRZ 60201-0408/1
L1323: This is the sponsor's presentation of the trial design.
L1324: **Title:** Prospective, double-blind, placebo controlled, randomized, multicenter trial with a double blind parallel group extension period (408/2) to investigate the efficacy and safety of different doses of NT 201 in the treatment of cervical dystonia.
L1325: ## Phase 3
L1326: **Purpose:** To investigate the safety and efficacy of 240 units dose and the 120 units dose of NT 201 compared with placebo in subjects with Cervical Dystonia as well as to compare the efficacy and tolerability of the two doses of NT 201.
L1327: **Trial design:** Prospective, multicenter trial, randomized, double blind, placebo controlled parallel group (240 units group and 120 units group), single treatment.
L1328: ## Endpoints:
L1329: ## **Primary endpoint:**
L1330: The primary efficacy variable was the change from Baseline to Week 4 (Visit 3) following injection in the TWSTRS Total Score, in the Intent to Treat (ITT) Population. This was similar to the endpoint used in the studies for approving Dysport®. In contrast, the studies on Botox® used the Cervical Dystonia Severity Scale which was devised for the Botox study combined with Physician Global Assessment Scale.
L1331: - TWSTRS Total score is the sum of the TWSTRS Severity, Disability and Pain Score and ranges from 0 to 85 points where higher scores indicate greater impairment.
- The TWSTRS total score was to be carried out at each visit and the change from baseline was to be obtained by computing the difference between the TWSTRS score at baseline and at each visit.
L1332: ## **Analysis of Primary Endpoint:**
L1333: - **Analysis Populations:** All efficacy analyses will be performed on the ITT population and TPP population.**Intent to Treat (ITT) Population:** all randomized subjects were included in the analyses of each endpoint parameter. This was the primary analysis population.
- **Treatment per Protocol (TPP) Population:** Identical to the ITT population but excluding subjects with identifiable protocol
L1334: deviations; this population was also used for the sensitivity analyses.
L1335: ## • **Methodology:**
L1336: - First analysis will occur after completion of the Main Phase. The blind was broken in order to proceed to statistical analysis of results after the BDRM (blind data review meeting) and after the database was closed for the Main Phase.
- Second analysis will occur when 100 subjects will have completed the Extension Phase with an observation period of at least 48 weeks for the 120-day safety update that will be sent to the FDA after the NDA has been filed.
L1337: ## • **Randomization:**
L1338: - Each subject received one intramuscular injection of the investigational product or placebo. Subjects were randomized to the 240 U, 120 U or the placebo group. As the identity of the study medication was not provided, the treatment assignment remained blinded from patients and study site personnel.
- Depending on the treatment arm, the trial medication for a particular patient consisted of 4 vials with 100 U NT 201 (240 units), 2 vials with 100 units NT 201 and 2 vials with placebo (120 U group or 4 vials with placebo (Placebo group)).
- The same subject number assigned to a patient at V2 (baseline visit) was used for both the Main and Extension phases of the trial. The patients from the Main Phase were re-randomized at the start of the extension phase to 120 or 240 units of IncobotulinumtoxinA in a double-blind fashion. Randomization was performed using RANCODE version 3.6 which ensured blocked randomization by centers. The duplicates of randomization lists were sealed and stored in a safe in the Total Quality Management Department of Merz Pharma. They were not accessible prior to database close of the respective trial period
L1339: ## • **Handling method for missing data include:**
L1340: - Missing value replaced by baseline value
- Observed Cases
- Missing value replaced by Group Mean
L1341: ## **Primary Efficacy Parameters:**
L1342: - Primary efficacy parameter is the change from baseline Visit 2 (Day 0) in the TWSTRS total score to control visit 3 (week 4). Confirmatory analysis will be performed for the difference between the treatment groups in the ITT population.
- Confirmatory analysis of the primary efficacy parameter will be based on the comparison of least square means from an analysis of
L1343: covariance (ANCOVA) model at Visit 3 (Week 4) between treatment groups. ANCOVA will be used to define the model for the primary efficacy analysis. The dependent variable is the change from baseline of TWSTRS Total score and the independent variables are treatment, baseline TWSTRS Total score, gender, age, pre-treatment of cervical dystonia and pooled center.
L1344: - Comparisons between treatment groups will be performed by using a fixed sequence test procedure (step downward) in the ITT Population.The first hypothesis of the first step downward test procedure is the comparison between the NT 201 Regular Effective Dose Group (240 units) and placebo regarding least square means for the change from baseline in the TWSTRS Total score.
- If the null hypothesis has to be rejected, the second test procedure will be performed to compare the NT 201 MED (minimal effective dose = 120) with placebo.
- A further test between NT 201 240 U (or regular effective dose) group and NT 201 120 U (or minimal effective dose) group will investigate the difference between the 2 active treatments.
- Three different models will be presented:Full model with all adjusting variables used for confirmatory testing
- Final model with all adjusting variables with an influence of p \le 0.2 on the model
- Simple model including only treatment effect used as sensitivity analysis.
- All tests of the fixed sequence test procedure will be performed two sided with a type I error of \alpha=0.05.
- Sensitivity will be performed to assess the influence of the data on the primary efficacy outcome
L1345: ## Secondary Endpoints:
L1346: - The secondary efficacy variables were change from baseline in the TWSTRS Total score at all other post baseline visits...Change from baseline in TWSTRS Disability, Severity and Pain Scores were carried out at baseline and at each post baseline visit.
- TWSTRS severity scale ranges from 0 to 35 points and consists of the following ratings: Maximal Excursion (rotation, lateroanterocolls/retrocollis), Duration factor, Effect of sensory tricks, Shoulder elevation, Range of motion and Time factor.
- TWSTRS Disability scale is an assessment of the degree to which daily activities may be affected by cervical dystonia. Each category is reported on a 6 point scale and total disability scores ranges from 0 to 30 points (higher scores indicate greater impairment. The categories of the questions include work
L1347: performance (job or domestic), activities of daily living (feeding, dressing, hygiene), driving, reading, watching TV and leisure activities at home.
L1348: - TWSTRS Pain scale was used as part of the inclusion criteria it measures the subject's pain during the week prior to the study visit. The TWSTRS Pain scale ranges from 0 to 20 points and is the sum of a pain severity component, a duration component and assessment of the contributing pain to disability. The pain severity component ranges from 0 to 10 points with "0" meaning no pain and "10" the most excruciating pain imaginable". The subject rates the severity of neck pain experienced during the past week with three different grades: the best, the worst, and the usual pain status. The value of the pain severity component is calculated as:
L1349: \text{Severity} = \frac{[(2 \times \text{usual}) + \text{best} + \text{worst}]}{4}
L1350: - Pain duration component: Subject rates the duration of neck pain on a 6 point scale
- Contribution of pain to disability: Subject rates the degree to which pain contributes to disability on a 6 point scale
- Patient Evaluation of Global Response (PEGR) at Visit 5 (final visit)PEGR scale is a descriptive subjective 9 point response scale including the following:Very marked worsening (-4)
- Marked worsening (-3)
- Moderate worsening (-2)
- Slight worsening (-1)
- Unchanged (0)
- Marked Improvement (+1)
- Moderate improvement (+2)
- Slight improvement (+3)
- Complete abolishment of signs and symptoms (+4).
L1351: ## Secondary Efficacy Parameters
L1352: - For the secondary and tertiary parameters, the sponsor did not adjust for multiplicity nor did they identify a priori any secondary or tertiary endpoints for inclusion in the product label.
- TWSTRS Subscale scores (Disability, Severity and Pain) will be analyzed at Control Visit 3 (week 4) of the Main Period using the same analysis procedures used above in the TWSTRS Total score. All three treatment comparisons will be tested in the ANCOVA models as described for the primary efficacy endpoint above and the evaluations will be conducted for the ITT population...
L1353: - Patient Evaluation of Global Response (PEGR) will be analyzed at The Final Visit (Visit 5) of the Main Phase using an ANCOVA procedure in the ITT population. Missing data of this endpoint will be set to 0.
L1354: ## Tertiary Endpoints:
L1355: - Duration of Effect Measurement VariablesTime to onset of treatment: Time (days) from injection (Day 0) to the subjective estimation of the onset of effect. The subject was asked if he/she felt whether there had been a treatment effect at all telephone contacts/visits until Visit 4 (Week 8). If the subject answered “yes”, he/she was asked at which day after the injection the effect had occurred.
- Time to waning of treatment effect after injection (subjective): Time (weeks) from injection session (Day 0) to the subjective estimation of the waning of effect. Subjects who reported an onset of treatment effect were asked at each visit and telephone contact, starting on Week 4 at the earliest up to the Final Visit of the Main Period if he/she felt whether there had been a waning of treatment effect. If the subject answered “yes”, he/she was asked at which week after the injection the waning of effect had occurred.
- Duration of Treatment Effect: The duration of treatment effect is defined as time period from the day of injection until the time point of a new injection.
- Global Assessment of efficacy by investigator: The Investigator performed a global assessment of the efficacy of study treatment at Final Visit of the Main Phase on a 4 point Likert scale, with the following items being available: “very good”, “good”, “moderate” and “poor”
L1356: ## Safety endpoints:
L1357: - Adverse Events and baseline complaints
- Standard clinical chemistry and hematology
- Vital signs, body weight
L1358: ## Safety Analysis:
L1359: - Interim Safety Analysis will be performed for the first 100 completers of the Extension Period (for the 120-day safety update).
- Evaluable for Safety (EFS) Population, which is all subjects who received any amount of trial medication during the main phase, will be included in the safety analysis.
L1360: ## **REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:**
L1361: - The Agency had recommended use of the total TWSTRS instead of the Severity component. The Severity Component of the TWSTRS was used as the primary endpoint in the study done in Europe. Because the company has a claim for pain in their label, analysis of the TWSTRS Pain scores is important in this study.
- The secondary endpoints of Pain, Disability and Severity are components of the TWSTRS total (primary endpoint) and do no provide any added information to the TWSTRS total score since they are both merely subscales of the TWSTRS.
- The PEGR would have provided more information about patient response correlating it to efficacy of the drug if it was obtained at the primary endpoint visit (Week 4, around the peak of the first injection).
- The duration of treatment effect is an important tertiary endpoint because it tells us when the drug had passed its peak which may provide a rough estimate of when a patient needs injections. It would have been a more effective measure of need for injection if patients were followed up at least every week after they have reported that they need injection up to the time that they are actually injected.
- Because there was no formal statistical plan for the secondary endpoints, and no plan to deal with multiplicity, we regard these endpoints to be exploratory in nature.
L1362: ## **Inclusion**
L1363: - Male/Female age 18 to 75 years
- Clinical Diagnosis of Cervical dystonia of predominantly rotational form
- TWSTRS total score &gt; 20, TWSTRS severity &gt; 10, TWSTRS Disability &gt; 3, TWSTRS Pain &gt; 1
- On a stable dose of other medications (if any) used for focal dystonia treatment (e.g. anticholinergics and benzodiazepines) for at least 3 months prior to and expected throughout the Main Period.
- For pretreated subjects only: There should be documentation of the last two consecutive injection sessions with Botulinum toxin and stable therapeutic response directly prior to trial entry. The subject must have at least 10 weeks duration between the last injection with botulinum toxin for cervical dystonia and baseline visit and most recent injection with botulinum toxin must have been a maximum of 300 units of type A or 12000 units of type B.
L1364: ## Exclusion
L1365: - Traumatic torticollis or tardive torticollis
- TWSTRS-Severity score for anterocollis&gt;2 (pure anterocollis)
- TWSTRS Severity score for retrocollis&gt;2 (pure retrocollis)
- Myotomy or denervation surgery in the affected muscles
- Hypersensitivity to human serum albumin, sucrose or Botulinum toxin Type A
- Diagnosis of myasthenia gravis, Lambert Eaton syndrome, amyotrophic lateral sclerosis or any other significant neuromuscular disease which might interfere with the trial
- Current swallowing disorder of any origin (dysphagia scale&gt;3=severe, with swallowing difficulties and requiring a change in diet)
- Marked limitation on passive range of motion that suggests contractures or other structural abnormality, e.g. cervical contractures or cervical spine syndrome
- Treatment with Botulinum toxins for any indication other than cervical dystonia within 4 months prior to Baseline and during the trial
- Known current alcoholism or other drug abuse/dependence
- Severe or uncontrolled systemic disease (cardiac, renal pulmonary, hepatic, gastrointestinal, malignant tumor or know HIV infection)
- Present or pre-existing blood coagulation disorders including therapeutic or prophylactic anticoagulation
- Infection in the area of planned injection points or systemic infection presenting a hazard for local injections into the neck muscles
- Planned elective surgery under general anesthesia during the trial
- Nursing mothers
- Severe impairment of cognition or communication that in the opinion of the Investigator does not enable the subject to understand the Informed Consent and participate in the trial
- Patients who are currently involved or were involved within the past three months in another therapeutic research study.
- Previous participation in this clinical study
L1366: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1367: - The inclusion and exclusion criteria stated by the sponsor are appropriate for trial entry and are likely to adequately represent the typical patient that may receive treatment with IncobotulinumtoxinA if it is approved by the FDA. They are the same as the inclusion/exclusion criteria in the European study 0013 except that 408/1 includes naïve patients in the study.
L1368: ## Concomitant Medication
L1369: ## • **Prohibited medications**
L1370: - Botulinum toxin of any serotype in any body region four months prior to trial entry
- Concomitant treatment with phenol or alcohol injections
- Treatment with intrathecal baclofen was not allowed within two weeks prior to screening and during the maintenance phase of the trial
- Parenterally administered drugs that interfere with neuromuscular transmission (tubocuraries, volatile anesthetics, barbiturates)
- Aminoglycoside antibiotics of any dosage and route of administration (e.g. amikacin, gentamicin, netilmycin, spectinomycin, tobramycin)
- Antimycobacterials (streptomycin, dihydrostreptomycin)
- Other antibiotics like lincomamide, tetracyclines and polymyxin
- Aminoquinolines (chloroquine and hydroxychloroquine)
- Anesthetics (including those locally applied in the area anticipated for treatment)
- Drugs that interfere with neuromuscular transmission: phenothiazine/neuroleptics like chlorpromazine, trifluoperazin, prochlorperazin, verapamil, dantroline or quinine
L1371: ## • **Permitted Medications**
L1372: - Any concomitant medication that was not related to the treatment of cervical dystonia
- Non steroid anti inflammatory drugs for pain relief
- Peripheral muscle relaxants are to be used with caution. Intermediate action agents were to be used in preference to those with longer lasting effects and starting dose was to be reduced.
- These medications are allowed if doses are stable for at least 3 months prior to entry and during the course of the Main phase. Changes in the doses of these medications were allowed in the Extension PhaseAnti depressive medications of any route of administration
- Opioid analgesics
- Oral medications for treatment of focal dystonia: anticholinergics like trihexyphenidyl, benztropine, ethopropazine/profenamin, tiapride, procyclidine
- Benzodiazepines
L1373: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1374: - The medications used for the symptomatic treatment (and used off label) have been listed above in the Sponsor's protocol. There has been no mention of other anti-epileptics such as Gabapentin that can reduce pain or somehow cause muscle relaxation. These medications are important because taking these medications at a period following injection may affect the efficacy data by providing relief. If there is an imbalance in the
L1375: treatment assignment of patients taking concomitant medications it could bias towards the treatment groups. Analysis of the sponsor's data on concomitant medication use for patients in study 408/1 did not find that there was a significant imbalance in the amount or type of concomitant medication use between patients enrolled in the 3 treatment arms.
L1376: ## **Subject Enrollment:**
L1377: - Planned: 222 subjects (N=74 per treatment group of MP with at least 87 subjects (40%) treatment Naïve
- Analyzed for MP:N=233 (81 in group 240 U, 78 in group 120 U and 74 in placebo group) for safety and ITT population (including 90 naïve subjects)
- N=215 (77 for 240 U, 70 for 120 U, 68 for placebo) in TPP population with 89 naïve subject
L1378: **Sample Size Estimation:** The sample size was based upon a 2 sided t test at \alpha = .05 level. A study with N=49 completing subjects per group has 90% power to detect a treatment difference of 4 points for placebo and of 10 points for 240 U group with a common standard deviation of 9 points for the mean change from baseline in TWSTRS total score. The sample size was increased to 59 subjects per group to increase the database size for safety assessments and to increase the power for demonstrating a difference between 120 units group and placebo. Assuming a 20% dropout rate, a target of 74 subjects per treatment group was required, with a total sample size of 222 subjects.
L1379: ## **Protocol Deviations:**
L1380: - Defined as deviations from the procedure outlined in the protocol.
- Identified for each subject during the respective Blind Data Review Meeting for the MP/EP.
- Assessed as “minor” or “major” depending on its impact on efficacy results.
- Protocol deviations were fully documented prior to unblinding.
L1381: ## **Treatments and other ancillary management**
L1382: The protocol did not prespecify the amount of study medication to be injected per muscle or the number of injections. The protocol described the total volume (4.8 ml) of the study drug to be injected, which was equal for all 3 treatment arms. The instruction was “The Investigator must rely on a careful physical and neurological examination of the patient, as well as previous experience to select the dystonic muscles” for treatment. In addition, a table of muscles frequently involved in stereotypic abnormal head positions was provided to help the investigator determine which muscles to inject with the study drug. The Investigator also determined the number of injection sites and the dose at each site. The maximum volume of IncobotulinumtoxinA that could be administered in a single injection could not exceed 1.0 to 1.2 ml. A table of drug exposure of NT 201 (U) by muscles used in Patients with Cervical Dystonia was provided by the sponsor as a guide. The protocol included a statement that “an injection
L1383: with more than 100 U in the sternocleidomastoid (SCM) and a bilateral injection in the sternocleidomastoid muscles should be avoided to decrease the risk of dysphagia. Additionally, an injection in “the upper third of the sternocleidomastoid will minimize the risk of dysphagia”. Most patients received well below 100 units in the SCM during the Main and Extension Phases of the trial.

L1388: ## Subgroup Analysis:
L1389: **Demographic and Baseline Characteristics:** Data will be compared among the three treatment groups with descriptive statistics and frequency tables.
L1390: **Naïve vs. Pre-treated Subjects:** Subgroups analysis of treatment naïve and pretreated patients will be performed for all efficacy and safety analyses in both the main and extension phase.
L1391: - **Treatment naïve subjects:** All randomized subjects who did not receive any prior treatment with botulinum toxin of any serotype.
- **Pre-treated subjects:** All subjects of the EFS population who received prior treatment with Botulinum Toxin of any serotype.
L1392: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L1393: The following Amendments were dated March 24, 2006:
L1394: - Start date changed from March 2006 to July 2006.
- Enrolment of 222 patients with at least 87 patients (40%) as treatment naïve. The previous protocol only reported enrolling 198 treatment naïve and pretreated patients and there was no mention of how many patients will be treatment naïve.
- An amendment allowed dose reduction in case of essential safety concerns in the Extension Period.
- All analysis will be performed on the ITT as well as TPP population and all safety analyses of the Main Period will be performed on the subgroups of treatment naïve and pre-treated patients.
- In the case in the EP that 240 U of NT 201 would represent an essential safety concerns, the volume of 4.8 ml can be reduced.
- Patients counted as treatment naïve in the Extension Period were all treatment naïve patients who participated in the Main Period and who were randomized to the placebo group. These patients will be handled as treatment naïve in the extension in the Extension Period until they receive the 2nd injection during Extension Period.
- All efficacy analyses will be performed in the subgroups, the ITT and TPP population.
- Safety analysis will be performed for the whole sample as well as for treatment naïve and pre-treated patients separately for Main Period and Extension Period.
L1395: The following amendments were made on April 18, 2007
L1396: - The trial will be performed in approximately 40 centers (as opposed to 35 centers).
- Treatment periods with any type of Botulinum toxins prior to screening should be recorded. Certain previous medications or treatment, if applied within the time frame in the exclusion criteria, will prevent a patient's enrollment in the study.
L1397: ## 6 REVIEW OF EFFICACY
L1398: ## Efficacy Summary
L1399: ## 6.1 Indication
L1400: In the proposed label submitted by the sponsor, the drug Xeomin® is indicated (b) (4)
L1401: i. This study investigated the efficacy, safety and tolerability of two doses of NT 201 (240 U and 120 U doses) compared to placebo in subjects with Cervical Dystonia. The study involved 37 centers in the United States, enrolling 233 patients from July 24, 2006 to March 19, 2008. Its Extension Phase (408/2) is ongoing with 24 blinded patients. The indications written in the label for both Botox A® and Dysport® are similar to the indications proposed by Xeomin®.
L1402: This is a randomized, double blind, placebo controlled, single treatment study which measured the TWSTRS total score, the primary efficacy parameter, at week 4 (visit 3) after baseline injection. Secondary efficacy variables obtained are the subscores of the TWSTRS total which include Disability, Severity and Pain. A Patient Evaluation of Global Response at the Final Visit of the Main Phase was done. Tertiary efficacy parameters include duration of effect, time to onset and time to waning of treatment after injection. The choice of measuring the efficacy parameter at the 4th week coincides with the peak of effect of the dose as seen in studies for the other botulinum toxins. The Sponsor also lists relief of pain in the desired indication and the TWSTRS Pain subscore was the only data used to support this claim. The result from these subscore was not replicated with another validated rating scale for pain such as the VAS. Although the tertiary treatment efficacy parameters rely on subjective reporting, including these parameters in the Sponsor's protocol will provide a rough estimate on the frequency of administration of the drug. Each of the primary and secondary efficacy parameters were evaluated in different subgroups (gender, pre-treated and naïve patient).

L1407: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS
L1408: - In clinical trial 408/1, the mean age was 52.8 years (17% or 39 patients were 65 years old and above). This is consistent with the age of occurrence of cervical dystonia. There is a predominance of females (66%) enrolled in the study compared to males, which is consistent with the data from previous epidemiologic studies (Nutt, JG, et al. Mov Disord 1988; 3:188-194) in Cervical dystonia reporting a 2:1 female predominance over males.
- Two hundred thirteen patients were Caucasians and 20 were non Caucasians (Asians, African American, Hispanic or Latino and American Indian). This difference is not expected to affect the safety or efficacy outcome of the study as incidence of Cervical dystonia has been reportedly higher in white individuals (1.23/100,000) than other races (0.15/100,000) (Tanner CM, et al Neurology. 2007, 69:676-680).
L1409: - The median weight was slightly higher by 19 lbs in the 120 U group (179 lbs) than in the 240 U group (160) and placebo group (165 lbs). This is not explained by the gender distribution of the groups as there are equal number of males and females in each treatment arm. The BMI (affected by weight) is also higher in the 120 U group.
- There were a total of 39 patients who were 65 years or older. The oldest patient enrolled in the study is 79 years old. Only 25 patients over the age of 65 were treated with IncobotulinumtoxinA (14 patients received 240 U and 11 patients received 120 U). There were too few patients age 65 or older enrolled in the study to make any inference regarding tolerability and safety of IncobotulinumtoxinA in this patient population.

L1427: ## **B. Concomitant Medications**
L1428: The table of concomitant medications (below) taken by the patient at the time of enrolment into study 408/1 and 408/2 is important because some of the medications might alleviate the symptoms of CD and this effect may be attributed to IncobotulinumtoxinA. Important medication classes in this table are the anti-inflammatory, analgesics, psychoanaleptics, antiepileptics, psycholeptics and muscle relaxants. These treatments have been used (although not FDA approved) to relieve some of the symptoms of cervical dystonia (including pain). The protocol required that the patients be maintained on stable dosages of these medications prior to trial entry to prevent the effect of these drugs in influencing the efficacy endpoints for IncobotulinumtoxinA.

L1436: ## 6.1.3 Subject Disposition
L1437: There were three hundred one subjects (301) enrolled from 37 centers in the United States. Sixty eight patients were excluded for screen failures (most common reasons are listed in the table below). Only 233 (77%) of patients were randomized and treated and 219 (94% of randomized patients) completed the Main Period of the study. Site 1017 was the highest enroller (31 subjects) and also had the highest number of screen failures.

L1439: The diagram below shows subject disposition, including the number of patients who had withdrawn from each randomized group.
L1440: ## **REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:**
L1441: - The sponsor analyzed all efficacy variables using the ITT population consisting of 233 subjects (81 in the 240 U group, 78 subjects in the 120 U group and 74 subjects in the Placebo group). Of these subjects, 219 (94%) completed the MP of the study and 14 (6%) withdrew from the study: Three subjects in the treatment group had adverse events: musculoskeletal pain, neck pain/muscle weakness and nausea and dizziness. Three (3) subjects in the placebo group withdrew due to insufficient efficacy, which was expected from the group. There were three patients who were lost to follow up (one in each treatment group) and 3 others who “withdrew consent”—and the reasons were not further explored by the Sponsor. The total number of patients who withdrew from the study was a small number and was equally distributed in the three treatment arms. The reviewer believes that these withdrawals did not affect the efficacy results nor did it contribute to any bias in one particular group.
L1442: ## **Protocol Violations:**
L1443: The table below lists all the major protocol deviations. The major protocol deviations are not mutually exclusive as subjects may have had more than one deviation. A total of 18 subjects in the ITT population were excluded from the TPP analysis due to major protocol violations. The TPP analysis included 215 (92% of the ITT population) subjects (77 in the 240 U group, 70 in the 120 U group and 68 in the placebo groups). The most number of major protocol deviations (7 of 18 or 3%) occurred because of errors in the “administration of the study medication” in which subjects were injected with an incorrect amount (number of units or volume) of the drug or the drug was injected into the wrong muscle. Five patients (2.1%) had raters who were different in each visit or were untrained raters. There were 3 patients who were in the category “early termination”.

L1450: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1451: - There were minor deviations that were considered as major deviations by some centers. For example, "early termination" and "trained rater" were reported as minor protocol deviations in some centers while others have listed these as major protocol deviations leading to withdrawal of study subject. It is unclear from the data submitted why these types of violations were classified differently. These inconsistencies in the classification of major/minor protocol violations may have been overlooked by the Sponsor.
- The number of missing visits, assessments and study procedures, when combined, were the most common minor deviations. The number of minor deviations was roughly
L1452: distributed among the three treatment groups. The number of missing assessments is too small to have affected the efficacy analysis
L1453: - One minor deviation that was listed by the Sponsor is “non-authorized concomitant treatment”. In this category, 33 (14%) patients in the 120 U group and 35 (15%) patients in the 240 U group compared to 25 (11%) patients in the placebo group have been found to use a non-authorized concomitant treatment. Relief from such medications may improve CD symptoms and confound the TWSTRS score.
L1454: The drugs listed in the table below have been reported (case reports or anecdotally) to alleviate or contribute to the alleviation of symptoms of Cervical Dystonia. These are not FDA approved for the treatment of CD and were used by patients in the study. (The Sponsor has provided a list of pain medications only and the reviewer supplemented this list with other medications used in clinical practice for “treatment” of CD). Although the percentage difference is not too large, the fact that these medications were seen more in the treatment groups (29%) than in the placebo seems to be favorable to the treatment groups.

L1458: ## 6.1.4 Analysis of Primary Endpoint(s)
L1459: ## General Analysis
L1460: - All efficacy variables were analyzed for the ITT population and for the TPP population. Confirmatory efficacy analysis was based on the ITT population. The sponsor presented analyses for the ITT population and TPP population was discussed only if different from the ITT population
- The primary efficacy variable was the change from Baseline (Visit 2, Day 0) in the TWSTRS Total score to Visit 3 (Week 4) following injection of the study medication.

L1475: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1476: - Using the full model, there is a treatment difference in the primary efficacy variable, which is the change from baseline (Visit 2, Day 0) in the TWSTRS Total Score to Control Visit 3 (Week 4) as shown in the tables 18, 19 and 20 (above). Regardless of the imputation method for handling missing data, the mean difference in TWSTRS total score in the 240 U group and the Placebo group ranged from -9 to -9.1 (p value &lt;0.001).
- Similarly, the change from baseline to visit 3 in the TWSTRS Total Score in the 120 U group and the placebo group ranged from -7.5 to 7.
- There was a slight mean difference of 1.3 to 1.4 units in the TWSTRS total score, between the 240 U group and 120 U group, favoring the 240 units but this did not reach statistical significance (0.425 to 0.447) even with different imputation methods for handling missing data.
- In the study, the sponsors presented detailed results using other sensitivity analyses employing different methods for handling missing data and different models that yielded very similar results. Consistently, treatment groups showed a statistically significant benefit over placebo and a non statistically significant small numerical difference between treatment groups, favoring the 240 U group.
- Additional information on Gender and baseline TWSTRS Total scores demonstrated that the mean improvement in TWSTRS score was generally higher in females especially those pre treated with botulinum toxin and who received 240 U. Although the “female gender advantage” is seen in the 240U group over placebo (with a p=0.01), the change in TWSTRS score between the two treatment groups is not as robust. Using gender as a covariate should not have been allowed in the study method mainly because he ANCOVA analysis pre-specified a high p-value (0.2) for covariates that included gender. Besides, there is no plausible biological reason to expect a significant difference in response to IncobotulinumtoxinA in women compared to men. The difference in pre-treated women is more likely explained by the larger number of women enrolled into the trial.
L1477: ## 6.1.5 Analysis of Secondary Endpoints(s)
L1478: The subscales of the TWSTRS (Severity, Disability and Pain) were analyzed at Visit 3 (Week 4) of the Main Phase. The evaluations were conducted for the ITT population.
L1479: Table 21 (below) shows the mean baseline value and change from baseline to visit 3 (week 4) and visit 4 (week 8) and also the final visit. This table demonstrates that the highest difference in TWSTRS severity score is during Visit 3, with the largest difference seen in the 240 units group (-5.5). Difference from baseline to visit 3 was also seen in the 120 U group (-3.9) and in the placebo group (-1.9) but the change is greatest with the treatment groups. Although the treatment effect may last up to 12 weeks, this effect wanes with time. There is almost no noticeable difference seen in the placebo group by the 12th week. This shows that the active treatment reaches a maximum effect at week 3 and wanes over time.

L1497: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1498: - Fifty four (67%) patients in the 240 U group and 50 (64%) patients in the 120 units reported slight improvement to complete abolishment of all signs and symptoms compared to 15 (20%) patients in the placebo group. There were more patients who received the 120 units group who reported complete abolishment of all signs and symptoms. Marked improvement was predominant in those who received 240 units. Although the number of patients who had experienced worsening was equal in both the 240 units and 120 units group, 4.9% reported marked worsening in the 240 units and none in the 120 units (perhaps because of adverse effects). Narrowing down the table above to those who had "moderate improvement to complete abolishment of symptoms" show a predominance of patients in the 240 U group compared to the 120 U group (53% in the 240 U group, 47% in the 120 U group compared to 11% in the placebo group) who reported at least a moderate degree of improvement.
- The Sponsor did not obtain the PEGR at Week 4 (Visit 3) at the time when the peak drug effect was expected and when the primary endpoint was measured. The PEGR ratings may have been influenced in the patients who experience peak dose adverse effects. At the last visit, dose dependent adverse effects may have resolved weeks earlier and may not have been recalled when patients rated the PEGR at the last visit.
L1499: Table 26 (below) illustrates that the treatment effect measured on the PEGR was statistically significant compared to placebo, however the 240 U group was not statistically superior to the 120 U group in a direct comparison. The trial results did not demonstrate a meaningful (reported by patients on the total TWSTRS Score or the PEGR) advantage of the 240 U treated group compared to the 120 U group.

L1514: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1515: - The onset, waning and duration of treatment tertiary variables may provide supporting information regarding the mean duration of treatment effect
- Generalizations about duration of effect, frequency of treatment, safety and efficacy of such treatment can only be made after patients receive repeated injections in the Extension Phase. While the mean duration of treatment is about 78.1 to 79.4 days, review of records show that the first injection in the Main Phase till the first repeat injection in the Extension phase ranged from as early as 42 days and up to 269 days (mean = 72.09 and median 58). Part of this can be explained by the Sponsor's protocol allowing injections as early as 6 weeks since the first injection and up to 48 weeks. This may explain the large range of variability in the treatment duration. More information about duration of treatment will be discussed in the Extension Phase (408/2).
L1516: ## 6.1.7 Analysis of Clinical Information Relevant to Dosing Recommendations
L1517: - Both 240 U and 120 U are superior to placebo for the treatment of cervical dystonia demonstrating a statistically significant difference between the treatment and the placebo groups from the baseline TWSTRS total score to the evaluation four weeks after baseline injection (-9 and -7.5 point difference for the treatment group, 240 U and 120 U respectively). The treatment difference was maximum at week 4. There was also a statistically significant treatment effect of IncobotulinumtoxinA compared to placebo reported for TWSTRS severity, disability and pain subscales at week 4 and in the Patient Effective Global Response where slight to complete resolution of symptoms was reported by the patients at the Final Visit.
- While both 240 units and 120 units groups were superior to placebo at the Week 4 visit compared to baseline for the TWSTRS total and subscale scores, there is no significant benefit associated with the 240 U over 120 U in the over-all population and even among the subgroups.
- The muscle groups that were injected were not specified in the protocol. A guide was provided to the Investigators regarding muscles that are activated in cervical dystonia. The muscles that were injected, the dose given and the number of injections were decided upon by the investigators.
- The treatment groups received NT 201 in a volume of 4.8 ml. The dosing regimen was indicated in the protocol and there were a few dosing errors documented in the study report but none that were associated with a SAE.
L1518: With regard to drug administration, the protocol did not pre-specify the muscles to be injected or the dose to be injected in each muscle group. The study did not explore which dose ranges were the most efficacious and safest. Tables 30 and 31 below summarizes the extent of exposure to toxin per muscle in the 240U and 120U group and illustrate that there is a very wide range of doses injected in each muscle in this particular study. This collection of data cannot be used as an injection guide in clinical practice. In the study as in clinical practice, the dose to be injected is dependent on the muscle group, the bulk and action of the muscle, the patient's previous exposure to neurotoxins and the Investigator's experience in treating patients with CD

L1524: ## 6.1.8 Discussion of Persistence of Efficacy and/or Tolerance Effects
L1525: The mean time of onset based on the data presented is 4 to 8 days and the mean duration of treatment is 77 to 84.9 days which is close to 11 weeks. For Botox, the onset of action is about one to two weeks (Jankovic and Schwartz, 1990b) and maximum effect is 6 weeks post injection. Duration of effect is 3 months. Dysport peak response is documented at 2 to 4 weeks with peak symptomatic relief within two to 4 weeks after injection.
L1526: The discussion of the subsequent Extension Phase study 408/2 will include additional information of the persistence of efficacy and/or tolerance effects of NT 201.
L1527: ## 6.1.9. Other Supporting Studies
L1528: ## Study Protocol: MRZ 60201-0408/2
L1529: ## Objective:
L1530: To investigate the safety and efficacy of repeated injections of the two different doses of NT 201 in subjects with cervical dystonia over one year period.
L1531: ## Methodology:
L1532: Randomized, double blind, parallel group (240 units group, 120 units group, repeated treatment).
L1533: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENT:
L1534: - The patients were re-randomized into 240 U group or 120 U group following participating in the Main Phase. There were no adjustment in the doses made during subsequent visits and patients and physicians were blinded into the treatment group. Doses between 120 U to 240 U were not evaluated.
L1535: ## Duration of Treatment:
L1536: Up to 5 injection sessions; dosing interval depending on duration of treatment effect, but not shorter than 6 weeks and the last injection could not be given later than 48 weeks after start of EP. Follow up assessments were required at a maximum of up to 20 week safety observation phase without treatment but could occur earlier if reinjection was required.
L1537: ## Efficacy variables:
L1538: The Extension Phase will include TWSTRS Total Score and the Patient Evaluation of Global Response.
L1539: ## **Safety Variables:**
L1540: - Clinical Chemistry, Hematology
- Antibody titer
- Pregnancy test
- Adverse events
- Changes in concomitant medication/diseases
L1541: ## **Safety Monitoring:**
L1542: On going AE's at the trial termination visit will be followed until AE has resolved or has stabilized or until a new injection with Botulinum Toxin of any serotype other than study medication (documentation either by phone or personal contact).
L1543: ## **Inclusion**
L1544: - Completed the double blind portion of the main trial
- Negative Pregnancy test before injection
L1545: ## **Exclusion**
L1546: - Received another botulinum toxin
L1547: ## **Extension Period Procedure:**
L1548: - The Extension Period consists of a 48 (+1 week) double blind treatment period and a safety observation period of up to 20 weeks (+/- 3 days) after the last injection with NT 201.
- Patients immediately enter the Extension Period after completion of Visit 5 of the Main Period (Week 8 to Week 20).
- If patient requires a new injection and the Investigator agrees this is necessary, the patient can be treated immediately with NT 201.
- If patient does not still require a new injection, the first Extension Period visit will be scheduled no later than 48 weeks (+1) after the Final Visit of the Main Period.
- Mandatory telephone contact will be made on Day 7+/-1 after each injection and control visits will take place 4 weeks after each injection.
- During the 48 week extended treatment period, all patients will receive multiple injections as required.
- Minimum interval between two injections must always be at least six weeks.
- Trial Termination Visit will be at 20 weeks (+/-3 days after the last injection of the Extension Period or at the time point when the patient receives a new injection of Botulinum Toxin of any serotype other than study medication which are allowed after patient has finished the extended treatment period.
L1549: - Study staff will determine reason for non attendance if patient does not attend for a new injection for a period of 20 weeks (+/-3 days) following last injection.
L1550: ## Dose
L1551: The subjects were re-randomized into receiving 120 units and 240 units. However, the sponsor submitted a protocol amendment for allowing individual dose adjustment reduction if the Investigator believed a dose reduction was needed because of safety concerns. The Extension Phase of the study is ongoing and approximately 1/3 of the randomized subjects remain blinded (at the 120-day update). This reviewer is unable to determine how many patients in each dose group required dose adjustments.

L1555: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:
L1556: - While the study blind was maintained throughout the extension phase, the intervals between injections were varied and could be as early as 42 days (or 6 weeks) from the last injection but not later than 48 weeks (end of trial).
L1557: - Extent of exposure, Safety data and information regarding the maintenance of treatment effect can only be examined after the study is complete and the full data set is unblinded and analyzed. Questions raised while the study is ongoing:Did the patients who were randomized into the placebo and 120 U groups in the Main Phase develop more side effects when they were re-randomized to the 240 U in the extension phase? A positive answer to this would supplement the dose response adverse effects already seen in the preliminary analysis.
- Did the patients who were initially randomized to 240 units in the Main Phase and were switched to 120 U in the EP report a loss of efficacy? If there were no complaints of lack of efficacy or a significant worsening in the TWSTRS scores, then this would support the conclusion drawn from the placebo controlled MP data that the 240 U dose not provided an advantage over the 120 U..

L1572: - The table above demonstrates the following:Out of the 214 patients who had continued into the Extension Phase, only 168 (79%) patients (58 in the 240 U group and 58 in the 120 U group, 52 still blinded) had 4 consecutive injections; 90 patients (42%) had 6 injections, with a total of 37 patients receiving 240 units. - The mean TWSTRS scores of patients in these tables are the TWSTRS scores when they needed the injection at that visit (their worse TWSTRS scores). The mean TWSTRS scores do not vary much based on whether they received 240 U or 120 U except towards the last two visits (Visits 12 and 14) where the patients who had received 120 U had lower mean TWSTRS total scores at the time when they “needed” an injection.
- Unfortunately, as previously mentioned this review would be unable to determine who among the patients in the Extension Phase received higher or lower doses than what they have received in the Main Phase and how this change in dose contributes to the efficacy of the drug.

L1575: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENT:
L1576: - There is a wide range of variability between the need for injections. The earliest need for injection was at 42 days after baseline treatment and the longest was 269 days from baseline injection. The need for earlier injection among pre-treated patients suggests increased tolerability of the medication.
- There is not much difference in the number of days between injections among patients who had received 120 U compared to those who received 240 U. Higher doses of other botulinum toxin products have been reported to be associated with a longer duration of effect and more frequent peak dose adverse effects. The 240 U dose of Xeomin (NT 201) is not associated with a significantly longer duration of between injections suggesting the duration of action of the 240 U dose is not longer.

L1605: The detailed safety data from the Extension Phase will be discussed by Dr. Lisa Jones, MD, MPH. Additional safety comments are provided below.
L1606: ## **Safety**
L1607: ## **REVIEWER'S COMMENTS:**
L1608: - The study has not yet been completed and has reports a total of 58 patients received 4 consecutive injections for the entire year and from 37 patients who have received at least 6 subsequent injections for the duration of a year in the 240 U group. Because of this limitation, further comments about safety with frequent dosing at higher doses cannot be made. The FDA may have to require the sponsor to submit PMC to account for more patients receiving higher doses of NT 201 with increasing dosing frequencies. Cervical dystonia, being a chronic disease, requires repeated injection and effects of repeated toxin exposure are important to assess safety of the drug.
L1609: ## **MRZ 60201-0013/1:**
L1610: The study has been included in this review because it provides additional safety data (as they have enrolled a larger number of subjects) and on the effect of IncobotulinumtoxinA on pain. Pain is a secondary efficacy parameter in this study and its results can support the data obtained from 408/1, although the sponsor did not identify Pain as a key secondary outcome and there was no correction for comparisons. It cannot be used though as a pivotal or supporting study primarily because its primary endpoint (TWSTRS Severity) is different from that in 408/1 (TWSTRS total). As it is designed as a non-inferiority (NI) study between IncobotulinumtoxinA and Botox, it does not have a placebo group comparison and lacks uncontrolled data for safety and assay sensitivity for effectiveness. It is also not adequately powered for non-inferiority. The sponsor did not seek advise from the agency regarding the design of this study or agreement on the NI margins.
L1611: **Title:** Safety and efficacy of NT 201 compared to BOTOX® in cervical dystonia.
L1612: **Investigators and Sites:** 51 main study investigators in 51 centers in 11 European countries
L1613: **Study Period:** March 2001 to April 2002 (Date of last completion)
L1614: **Phase of Development:** Phase 3
L1615: **Objective:** Safety and efficacy of NT 201 compared to BOTOX® in predominantly rotational cervical dystonia.
L1616: ## **Methodology:**
L1617: Randomized, double blind active controlled parallel group, multicenter trial on non inferiority. The drugs were administered by intramuscular injection in a 1:1 ratio, one unit NT 201 is regarded as being equal to one unit Botox.
L1618: - Dose Selection: As specified in the protocol submission, "prior to trial, the patient was to have received two successful BOTOX® treatments following the same injection scheme. "Successful treatment should have been demonstrated and the injected muscles, volume, number of injections and sites had to be recorded. At the baseline visit, patients were treated in a randomized double blind order with NT 201 or BOTOX® following the two consecutive treatments with BOTOX® prior to trial entry with a range of treatment ranging from 70 to 300 units of BOTOX®.
L1619: ## **Number of Patients:**
L1620: - Planned: 460 patients randomized including 10% dropouts, 412 patients treated per protocol
- Analyzed: 463 patients ITT, 420 patients TPP and 463 patients EFS
L1621: ## **Diagnosis and main criteria for inclusion:**
L1622: - Patients with cervical dystonia (rotational form) who needed injection
- Male/Female outpatients &lt;75 years old with last injection session with BOTOX® at least 10 weeks prior to randomization and willingness to participate as documented by written informal consent
- TWSTRS severity score &gt; 10, for rotation &gt; 2,
- Stable therapeutic response to BOTOX® in the last 2 injection sessions prior to trial entry receiving a dose of 70 to 300 units
- Stable dose of other focal dystonia therapy for three months prior to trial entry
- Negative pregnancy test and reliable means of contraception at trial entry for childbearing females
L1623: ## **REVIEWER'S COMMENTS**
L1624: - The inclusion criteria allowed patients enter if their last injection with BOTOX® was at least 10 weeks prior to randomization. This is less than the recommended duration of injection specified in the BOTOX® label (3 months) when patients should return for pre-treatment. The shorter interval since their last Botox dose may make either drug seem more efficacious.
L1625: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint:**
L1626: - Change in TWSTRS Severity score from baseline to control visit on day 28\pm3 after the injection of study medication
L1627: ## **Secondary Efficacy Endpoint:**
L1628: - TWSTRS Pain subscore, VAS pain and patient's evaluation of global response at control and final visit.
- Effect duration defined as interval between time of injection and final visit when TWSTRS severity score has reached at least 80% of baseline value or at the latest 112 days after injection
- Responder Analysis defined as an improvement of &gt; 20% from baseline in the TWSTRS Severity Score at the control visit
- Time to onset and time to waning of treatment effect after injection
- Investigator's global assessment efficacy
L1629: ## **Safety:**
L1630: - Dysphagia score, standard labs, vital signs, ECG, pregnancy test, adverse events, general physical and neuro exam, ELISA and HAD tests on BoNT/A antibodies
L1631: ## **Statistical methods:**
L1632: - Primary variable is the non-inferiority of efficacy of NT 201 compared to BOTOX®: based on change in Baseline TWSTRS severity using ANCOVA and 95% confidence
L1633: interval method in TPP. In the full model of the confirmatory analysis, the dependent variable was the change from baseline in the TWSTRS severity score and independent variables were treatment, baseline TWSTRS total score, gender, age, pretreatment of cervical dystonia and pooled center.
L1634: - Method of Imputation: No missing data was imputed.
- In the analysis of all efficacy variables, descriptive statistics were used for continuous variables and frequency tables for categorical data
- Kaplan Meier method was used for analyzing tertiary efficacy variables (Investigator's global assessment of efficacy) and log rank tests were used to analyze onset of disease, waning and duration of effect.
- Safety variables were analyzed using descriptive statistics, frequency and shift tables subgroup analysis stratification for age, gender, antibody tests. Kaplan Meier method was used to analyze the data was for adverse drug reactions and serious adverse events.
L1635: ## REVIEWER'S COMMENT:
L1636: - Although the non inferiority margin was chosen at \Delta=1.3, the rationale and the study that was used as a basis for coming up with this margin has not been specified in their study protocol. There is also no pre-specified M2 in their protocol.
L1637: ## Disposition of Patients:
L1638: - 51 centers in 11 countries recruited 466 patients and only 463 were randomized and constituted the ITT population (231 patients in NT 201 Group and 232 patients in the BOTOX® Group). There were 43 major protocol deviations (18 NT 201 and 25 BOTOX®) which resulted in a TPP Population of N=420 (213 patients in NT 201 group and 207 patients in the BOTOX® Group. Five NT 201 patients discontinued therapy with lack of efficacy as the most common cause of discontinuation. A total of 226 NT 201 patients completed the study.
- The most common reason for major protocol deviations includes lack of stable response and unavailable source documentation.
L1639: ## Demographics:
L1640: - Patient median in the NT 201 group was 50 years old.
- BMI was 24.8 kg/m2
- Estimated duration of cervical dystonia prior to entry was 9.4 +/- 7.7 in the NT 201 group
- Initial diagnosis prior to baseline visit was 7.5 +/- 6.9 years in the NT 201 group.
L1641: ## Efficacy Evaluation Summary:
L1642: In both treatment groups, the TWSTRS severity score was significantly reduced by about -6.6 for the IncobotulinumtoxinA group (p&lt;0.0001, ANCOVA) and -6.4 for the Botox (p&lt;0.0001 ANCOVA) group. These results are reported in the sponsor's tables 37 and 38 (below).

L1652: ## Results of other Secondary Parameters:
L1653: - VAS for pain: The two sample Univariate Wilcoxon test did not show any significant difference between NT 201 and BOTOX® treatment groups in the change from baseline in VAS pain score observed at the control and final visit.
- The global response of patients improved significantly (ANCOVA model) in both groups at control and final visits.
L1654: Anne E. A. Constantino, MD
L1655: NDA 125360-1
L1656: Botulinum Toxin A, Xeomin® IncobotulinumtoxinA
L1657: - There were no significant differences between groups with regard to the median time until onset of effect (7 days for each treatment group). Two treatment groups were comparable regarding time to onset (7 days after injection) and waning of effect (10 and 11 weeks for NT 201 compared to BOTOX®) and duration of treatment effect (110 days vs. 109.5 days for NT 201 vs. BOTOX®).
- There were no significant relevant differences between groups with regard to the median time until waning of effect (10 weeks for NT 201 and 11 weeks for BOTOX®).
- Duration of effect amounted to 110 days for NT 201 and 109.5 days for BOTOX®. The two treatment groups were comparable regarding duration of effect.
L1658: ## Safety Evaluation
L1659: - Overall, 65/231 (28.1%) patients treated with NT 201 reported 110 adverse events compared to 56/232 (24.1%) patients treated with BOTOX® who experienced 87 events.
L1660: A detailed Safety Evaluation of this study will be provided in the review of Dr. Lisa Jones, MD, MPH.
L1661: ## REVIEWER'S CONCLUSIONS ON STUDY 0013:
L1662: - This is an active comparator study of NT 201 and botulinum toxin (BOTOX®), using 70 to 300 units of NT 201 and botulinum toxin. The study enrolled only pre-treated patients. Enrolling only responders will not provide adequate safety data for toxin naïve patients. The study's primary endpoint is the TWSTRS severity score which is one of the components of the TWSTRS total score (used as primary endpoint in the pivotal study). The trial results of this study cannot be directly compared to the results of study 408/1.
- There were 144 females and 87 males who received NT 201 (ratio: 1:1.6) almost comparable to the gender ratio in the pivotal study. The mean BMI is 25.50.
- For the NT201 group, the mean TWSTRS severity score at baseline is 17.43. This is a little bit less than the TWSTRS severity score obtained in the pivotal trial (which was 18 for the 120 U group and 18.6 for the 240 U group). The mean TWSTRS severity score for IncobotulinumtoxinA group at baseline was 17.74 and the mean in 4 weeks is 15.98. The TWSTRS pain score in this study shows a -0.4 difference from baseline after four weeks, similar to the differences seen in 408/1.
- The results of the study showed non inferiority of NT 201 to BOTOX® but the rationale for the choice of the non-inferiority margin and the absence of a pre-specified M2 has not been provided by the Sponsor. Thus, it is hard to make a conclusion of non-inferiority based on the chosen delta because the quantitative effect of BOTOX is hard to determine. Since this study was done for the European regulatory authorities prior to starting the approval process in the United States, this statistical analysis plan was not presented to the FDA.
- Onset of effect for both drug treatments was 7 days after injection; waning of effect was 10 weeks for NT 201 and 11 weeks for BOTOX® and the duration of treatment effect was 110 days for NT 201 and 109.5 days for BOTOX®. The onset of effect in this study
L1663: is longer and the waning effect (subjective) was earlier than that documented in study 408/1.
L1664: - The total dose of study medication administered, mean and median, for all muscles in one injection session was 140.4 (120) compared to 138.9 (122.5) for BOTOX®. The NT201 dose range was from 50 U to 300 U. The most frequent dose was 100 U. Only 16 patients received a dose greater than 200 units. The doses given here were comparable to the doses given in the US except that the most frequent dose was 100 units which were 20 units less than those given in the United States and the 100 U still showed some benefit.
- Dysphagia and Pain were the most frequent adverse reactions. There is a slight difference in adverse events with more GI symptoms, especially dysphagia and pain occurring in patients given NT 201.
L1665: ## 7. Review of Safety
L1666: - A detailed safety analysis will be provided by Dr. Lisa Jones. MD, MPH.
L1667: *The safety population ranged from 8 to 236 patients (most number of enrollees in those with glabellar frown lines). Up to 300 units were used in cervical dystonia, 400 in spasticity and 100 in Blepharospasm.* (b) (4)

L1674: ## Safety Preliminary Conclusions: (Medical Safety Reviewer)
L1675: - There have been no new or unexpected safety signals with both doses of NT 201.
- Overall incidence of Treatment Emergent Adverse events was higher in the treated group, more in the 240 units than in the 120 units group. Neck pain and dysphagia were the most prominent TEAE's experienced by individuals in the study.
- Time to occurrence of adverse events was shortest (16.5, SD 21.03 days) in those who received 240 units than those who received 120 units (15.3, SD 18.37 days).
- Incidence of dysphagia was higher in those who received 240 units than those who received 120 units.
- In pre-treated subjects in the 240 units, percentage of subjects with mild swallowing difficulties was higher than baseline than those in the 120 units group.
- In treatment naïve subjects, likewise, those who received 240 units had more complaints of dysphagia with persistence of symptoms until the final visit.
L1676: Safety Review Comments by Dr. Lisa Jones (Safety Medical Reviewer):
L1677: I concur with the following comments of Dr. Lisa Jones:
L1678: - *"...the overall risk of common adverse event for CD patients is similar across doses: 57% in the 120 U group, 55% in the 240 U group and 42% in placebo. However, when restricted to common adverse events most likely to be caused by the drug (based on the frequency of events, the difference from placebo and past experience with other botulinum toxin products), these events appear to demonstrate a dose-response (For*
L1679: example dysphagia (13% 120U; 19% 240 U, 3% placebo), neck pain (6% 120 U, 15% 240 U, 4% placebo), muscle weakness (6% 120 U; 11% 240 U, 1% placebo).
L1680: I believe that there is a dose-response effect demonstrated within this study, with the 240 U dose having a greater risk of adverse events. I believe the reason that the risk for any non-serious AE are similar for the 120 U and 240 U groups is that the table above includes many incidental adverse events unlikely to be drug related (somnolence, sinusitis, syncope, etc) and these obscure the dose-response in the adverse events that are most likely to be drug related. “
L1681: - The adverse events listed above are more likely to be related to toxin injection, all are associated with a greater frequency in patients who are treated with 240 U compared to 120 or placebo. Of note is the nearly 20% of patients who reported experiencing dysphagia following treatment with 240 U of IncobotulinumtoxinA.”
L1682: ## Immunogenicity:
L1683: Part of the safety review is the relevance of Immunogenicity.
L1684: (b) (4)
L1685: (b) (4)
L1686: (b) (4) Immunogenicity becomes relevant because the treatment of focal dystonia require long term treatment requiring repeated injection. If neutralizing antibodies develop, patients may experience a permanent loss of effectiveness to all botulinum toxin type A products. A summary of the Sponsor's findings show that only four subjects, all pre-treated for cervical dystonia, became newly positive for neutralizing Botulinum toxin antibodies after injection of IncobotulinumtoxinA; however there were no reports of secondary treatment failure in these subjects.
L1687: (b) (4)
L1688: (b) (4)
L1689: (b) (4)
L1690: (b) (4) In practice, patients who report loss of benefit from a particular toxin product may not have detectable antibodies and that patients continue to demonstrate toxin responsiveness to a frontalis test. Other factors such as injection technique or dose may play a role in patients who report a loss of clinical effectiveness.
L1691: ## 8. Agency's Statistical Reviewer's Conclusions for Study 408/1
L1692: I concur with the conclusions of Dr. Ohidul Siddiqui. Here is his conclusion on the study:
L1693: Xeomin has demonstrated its efficacy in treating patients with cervical dystonia. In the study 408/1, the primary efficacy endpoint was the change in the TWSTRS total score from baseline to Week 4 post injection. In the ITT population, the difference between the Xeomin 240 U group and the placebo group in the change of the TWSTRS total score from baseline to Week 4 was -9.0 points; the difference between the Xeomin 120 U group and the placebo group in the change of the TWSTRS total score from baseline to Week 4 was -7.5 points. Comparison of each Xeomin group to the placebo group was statistically significant at p&lt;0.001. Subgroup analyses also revealed that the efficacy of the two Xeomin doses was similar in pre-treated subjects (i.e., subjects who had received a Botulinum toxin prior to this study) and in naïve subjects (i.e., subjects who had not received a Botulinum toxin prior to this study). Examination of age and gender subgroups did not identify differences in response to Xeomin among these subgroups. There were a few African-American subjects to adequately assess efficacy in that population.
L1694: ## 9. Clinical Reviewer's Conclusions
L1695: - IncobotulinumtoxinA is another type of botulinum toxin A that has been approved in Europe and other Latin American countries that seeks approval in the United States for the treatment of Cervical Dystonia. It differentiates itself from the other approved neurotoxins as it is highly purified (b) (4) (4)
- Clinical trial 408/1 attempted to demonstrate the efficacy and safety of two doses of IncobotulinumtoxinA in the treatment of cervical dystonia in a double blind, randomized study utilizing two preparations (240 units and 120 units) and comparing it to placebo. As confirmed by the Agency's statistical reviewer, the drug has demonstrated its efficacy in treating patients with cervical dystonia with a significant change in its primary efficacy endpoint, which is the Total TWSTRS score, 4 weeks post injection. Both formulations of 240 units group and 120 units group proved better than placebo with a statistically significant p at &lt;0.001.
- Subgroup analysis (in pre-treated and naïve subjects) also revealed the efficacy of the two dose formulations over placebo.
- In both treatment groups, the PEGR was better than placebo.
L1696: Anne E. A. Constantino, MD
L1697: NDA 125360-1
L1698: Botulinum Toxin A, Xeomin® IncobotulinumtoxinA
L1699: - However, there is no statistically significant difference between the treatment effect of 240 units and 120 units. The majority of the subgroup analyses and TWSTRS subscores favor the 240 units by a very small, non statistically significant margin. It is possible that this small difference may be due to chance mainly because of the following reasons in the randomization that may have been overlooked:The mean difference of the Weight and BMI of patients in the 240 units group is lower than that of the 120 units group and if these variables were equal in these two groups, a treatment difference would not be seen at all.
- More patients in the 240 units were using medications that may affect/relieve the overall symptoms of cervical dystonia and this could contribute to the small treatment effect difference seen in the two treatment groups.
L1700: These minor (non-statistically significant) differences between treatment groups are important to consider when looking at the treatment effect size between the two treatment groups.
L1701: - Merits of the 240 units over the 120 units in subgroups (women and pre-treated patients) are noted but are not consistent in all the efficacy variables. Likewise, the “gender effect”, where pre-treated women were seen to have benefitted more from the 240 units is not adequately supported because the statistical analysis used to obtain this result pre-specified a high p-value (0.2) for covariates that included gender.
- While 240 units may show more benefits than 120 units by a small numerical margin, there have been more TEAE's especially of dysphagia (prolonged, even if they were mild) in those who received higher doses. It should also be emphasized that there is a need to provide data for more than 60 patients who had repeated exposures to IncobotulinumtoxinA at its maximum dose of 240 U for a year in order to continuously assess safety.
- Whereas the 240 units dose is more effective than placebo, it is still advised that patients, regardless of whether they are pre-treated or treated, be started on 120 units of IncobotulinumtoxinA because of the dose response TEAE's seen in the higher doses of NT 201.
- Onset of effect, waning of treatment effect and treatment duration for both treatments groups was longer than placebo but there was no difference in the effects of 240 units over 120 units in any of these tertiary variables. The average number of weeks that can be used for recommendation of frequency of injections is 11 weeks.
- Although muscles injected and the doses given were documented in this study, choices of muscles to inject were dependent on the investigator. This implies that only physicians who have had experience with other botulinum toxins (or who have trained extensively with physicians who had experience with injections of botulinum toxins) should be the only ones administering this drug.
- There have been no studies done converting NT 201 to Dysport® or Myobloc®. Even the non-inferiority trial done in Europe comparing NT 201 and Botox does not have a strong basis for its non inferiority margin and cannot be used to make a decision on
L1702: Anne E. A. Constantino, MD
L1703: NDA 125360-1
L1704: Botulinum Toxin A, Xeomin® IncobotulinumtoxinA
L1705: whether the two drugs are bioequivalent and interchangeable. Its product label should include the statement that IncobotulinumtoxinA dose should not be used interchangeably with other type A botulinum toxin products.

L1714: ## 11.2 Labeling Recommendations
L1715: - The reviewer recommends 120 units (b) (4) as a starting dose in both pre-treated and naïve patients. In the clinical trial, patients were injected with up to 240 units but there was no added benefit to the higher dose. There have been no doses that were given in between 120 or 240 units. No studies were done on doses above 240 units in this clinical trial.
- In the study, the drug was given every 11 weeks and the drug may be given similarly every 12 weeks or 3 months.
- Conversion from one toxin to another is not allowed as there are no bioequivalence data comparing this drug with other drugs from its class.

L1742: Clinical Safety Review
