L2192: ## 7.2.3 Special Animal and/or In Vitro Testing
L2193: The sponsor performed several preclinical studies regarding cardiac function (including hERG channel screening) and animal studies addressing prenatal exposure. These studies are discussed in this review in Section 7.4.4.1 and Section 7.6.3 of this review, respectively.
L2194: Additional preclinical studies related to drug safety performed by the sponsor are summarized below12:
L2195: - **Genotoxicity:** No mutagenicity studies have been conducted in accordance with the guidance.
- **Carcinogenicity:** No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted, likewise in accordance with the guidance.
- **Single dose toxicity studies:** These were done in mouse and Sprague Dawley rats. The LD 50 ranged from 50 (given intravenously) to 100,000 (orally) with the maximum lethal dose causing death within 48 hours to 5 days. All animals at 150 LDU/kg died prematurely 3 to 8 days after injection and one treated with 50 LDU/kg died prematurely on day 5. There was also a decrease in body weight and body weight gain at doses of 50 and 150 LDU/kg. The calculated LD 50 of 41 and 87 LDU/kg was established for males and females respectively. The results indicated that 5 LDU/kg can be considered as the NOAEL for a single intramuscular injections of NT 201 in mice, however, there was no LD 50 determined on the IM administration. In male Sprague-Dawley rats, maximum paralysis score was reached by all animals from 48 to 96 hours after treatment at 32 and 64 LDU/kg.
- **Repeat dose toxicity studies:** Doses given above 10 LDU/kg every 2 to 4 weeks caused death when given intramuscularly in the rabbits. Repeat dose toxicity studies done in the cynomologus monkeys who were given doses as high as 12 LDU/kg in 4 weeks for 10 injections resulted in atrophied gastrocnemius muscle fibers. There were no treatment related effects on ophthalmology, cardiovascular function, food consumption, hematology, serum chemistry and urinary parameters. Based on this dose, the NOAEL for toxicity is &gt;4 times higher than the maximal recommended human dose.
- **Local tolerance toxicity:** No clinically relevant local injection site reactions after repeated intramuscular injection up to 40 LDU/kg or repeated intradermal administration up to 8.34 LDU/kg.
- **Locomotor activity:** The results were dose dependent and the reduction in motility parameters was observed starting at 20 LDU/kg. The NOAEL for motility parameters after a single IV injection is 9 LDU/kg which is approximately 1.3 fold greater than maximum clinical dose in humans (7.24 LDU/kg for 60 kg patient).
- **CNS Toxicity:** No specific studies were done to assess for CNS toxicity. Studies in the mouse at repeated doses with an approximate D 50 of 50 LDU/kg showed clinical signs of piloerection, ptosis, lacrimation, mydriasis, reduced motility, muscular tonus, ataxia, hypothermia and dyspnea.
L2196: 12 The description of some of the preclinical studies in this section were adapted from the review of NT 201 efficacy for the CD indication, prepared by Dr. Anne Constantino. The source information is from the sponsor's summary, and not from the FDA's Pharmacology-Toxicology review.
L2197: - **GI toxicity:** NT 201 did not show any effect on intestinal transit. The NOAEL of a single intramuscular injection of NT 201 for effects on gastro-intestinal motility in rats is 32 LDU/kg which is 4.5 fold greater than the maximum human clinical dose.
- **Dose-Response:** In the first study, the sponsor stated that after repeated administration of NT 201 to rabbits, no neutralizing antibodies were formed in the NT 201 group. In contrast, Merz stated that in each Botox group, at least 20% of animals developed neutralizing antibodies (Clinical Overview, pg. 15). A second relevant animal study comparing NT 201, Dysport® and Botox® found that the degree and duration of hind limb muscle paralysis in rodents was dose-dependent. Merz stated that the effects of the selected doses of NT 201 and Botox® were almost equipotent in terms of strength and length of the paralysis. The time to onset of paralysis was not found to be dose-related and the sponsor reported that onset did not differ between NT 201, Botox® and Dysport®. The maximal paralytic effect was reached within days after the injections with all three botulinum toxin preparations (Clinical Overview, pg. 14/Nonclinical Overview, pg. 8).
- **Time to Effect Onset:** Merz analyzed the paralytic effect of NT 201 and Botox® through electromyography (EMG) *in vivo* in Cynomolgus monkeys. NT 201 induced a maximum inhibition of EMG activity one week after treatment (Nonclinical Overview, pg. 5).
L2198: The sponsor also includes the following summary of animal testing in their proposed labeling (Labeling Section 13.2, Animal Toxicology and/or Pharmacology):
L2199: (b) (4)

L2224: ## 7.2.5 Metabolic, Clearance, and Interaction Workup
L2225: After systemic absorption, Merz explained that the NT 201 neurotoxin is rapidly metabolized to smaller molecular structures. White blood cells play a role in the disposal of circulating toxin type A as shown for other proteins. The neurotoxin is then degraded intracellularly. Like all proteins, Botulinum toxin type A is rapidly metabolized by proteases and molecular components are recycled through normal metabolic pathways.
L2226: Both bioavailability and storage ability of BoNT/A are affected by accompanying complex proteins. (b) (4) (b) (4) (b) (4)
L2227: Pharmacokinetic interactions between proteins and NT 201 after intramuscular injections are not expected.13
L2228: 13 This paragraph adapted from the CD efficacy review prepared by Dr. Anne Constantino.
L2229: ## 7.2.6 Evaluation for Potential Adverse Events for Similar Drugs in Drug Class
L2230: Within this review, adverse events that were evaluated in detail due to their occurrence in other botulinum toxin products are:
L2231: - Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESIs) as defined by a list created by the FDA and the sponsor during the pre-BLA period (see Section 7.3.5 of this review)
- Any symptom or pattern suggesting systemic spread (see Section 7.3.5.2 of this review)
- Hyperglycemia (See Section 7.3.5.3 of this review)
- Elevation in Alkaline Phosphatase (See Section 7.3.5.4 of this review)

L2234: ## 7.3.1.1 *Death Overview*
L2235: There were a total of six deaths in patients within the NT 201 development program for all indications (including the non-neurologic indications): two in patients treated with placebo, one in a patient treated with Botox® and three in patients treated with NT 201. The three deaths in the NT 201-treated patients all occurred within the spasticity indication studies, and all three were during the OLEX period.
L2236: **Reviewer comment:** The sponsor noted that Subject 1001 in Study 0607 (SP) experienced a fatal myocardial infarction during the screening period of the study prior to receiving drug treatment. The sponsor did not include this deaths in the list of analyzable deaths, and I agree with this exclusion.

L2244: ## 7.3.1.2 Deaths Narratives
L2245: There were six deaths in the NT 201 development program. Five occurred in the spasticity indication (three drug-treated and two placebo patients) and one in a Botox-treated patient within the CD indication. Abridged narratives for the six deaths are presented below.
L2246: - **Patient 1054 (Xeomin-treated, SP indication, Study 0410 OLEX):** This 72-year-old man with post-stroke spasticity participated in the Main and OLEX Periods of Study 0410. During the Main Period, he received a single treatment with NT 201 on June 23, 2006. The patient had a past medical history significant for ischemic stroke approximately six years earlier, arrhythmia, ventricular pacemaker, hypothyroidism, coronary artery disease, hypertension, and aortic aneurysm. He was also a former smoker. He had received six Botulinum toxin treatments before study entry. An ECG performed at Screening was abnormal (the subject had a ventricular pacemaker)..
L2247: At entry into the OLEX Period of the study, the patient's medications were digoxin, warfarin, levothyroxine, tamsulosin, ramipril, naftidrofuryl, and piracetam.
L2248: In the OLEX Period of the study, the subject received 375 U of NT 201 on January 15, 2007. On (b) (6) the patient was hospitalized and diagnosed with a pulmonary embolism, which was treated and resolved. On (b) (6), the subject experienced a cerebrovascular accident. His hospitalization was complicated by progressive apathy and malnutrition. He began to refuse oral medication. (b) (6), the subject died in his sleep of an unknown cause. A post-mortem examination was not performed.
L2249: **Reviewer comment:** This patient died following a cerebrovascular accident (CVA). Given that he had a previous CVA prior to entering the study, the occurrence of a second CVA is not unexpected, which considerably lessens, although does not exclude, the chance that the death was drug related.
L2250: 2. **Patient 1058 (Xeomin-treated, SP indication, Study 0410 OLEX):** This 67-year-old man with post-stroke spasticity participated in the Main and OLEX Periods of Study 0410. The patient had not been treated previously with Botulinum toxin. His past medical history was significant for hypertension, cerebrovascular accident approximately 2.5 years previously, hemiparesis and being a former smoker. An electrocardiogram performed at screening was assessed as normal.
L2251: In the OLEX period of the study, the patient received treatment with NT 201 on November 9, 2006 and March 15, 2007. On (b) (6), approximately six weeks after the last administration of NT 201, the patient died as a consequence of a “severe cardiac arrest.”
L2252: **Reviewer comment:** *The sponsor's narrative did not specify whether this cardiac arrest death was verified by autopsy or laboratory results. A request for information was sent to the sponsor, and the response is shown below:*
L2253: 1. **FDA Question:** For patient 1058 (Xeomin-treated, SP indication, Study 0410 OLEX) whose cause of death was attributed to “severe cardiac arrest,” was the cardiac arrest verified by either laboratory findings or autopsy?
L2254: **Merz Response:** Patient S1058 was a 68-year-old male subject who died of severe cardiac arrest six weeks after the last administration of NT201 study medication for post-stroke spasticity. It was reported that the patient's wife found her husband without any signs of life. She called the rescue service which stated death by cardiac arrest. As the patient was already dead upon arrival of the rescue services, no laboratory or diagnostic tests could be done. An autopsy was not performed (BLA Amendment 0019, dated February 4, 2010).
L2255: *The sponsor's reply confirms that no testing was performed to verify that this was a death due to cardiac arrest, and this attribution appears to have been made by the ambulance crew. I would consider the cause of death in this patient to be unknown, as the patient had a history of cerebrovascular events, and a catastrophic CVA or other causes of sudden death have not been ruled out.*
L2256: 3. **Patient 1169 (Xeomin-treated, SP indication, Study 0410 OLEX):** This 71-year-old man with post-stroke spasticity participated in the Main and OLEX Periods of Study 0410. The subject received the first dose of NT 201 on July 18, 2006. The subject's past medical history was significant for ischemic cerebrovascular accident approximately 4.4 years earlier, hypertension, and hemiparesis. He was also a former smoker. The subject had been treated for upper limb spasticity with Botulinum toxin before entering the study. An ECG performed at Screening was normal.
L2257: In the OLEX Period of the study, the patient received four injections of NT 201 on October 13, 2006, January 8, 2007, April 2, 2007 and June 25, 2007. On (b) (6), approximately (b) (6) after the last administration of NT 201, the subject suffered a
L2258: severe cerebrovascular accident at home and died as a consequence. A post-mortem examination was not performed.
L2259: **Reviewer comment:** The sponsor's narrative for Patient 1169 did not provide details on how the determination of CVA was made given that no autopsy was performed and the patient died at home. A request for additional information was sent to the sponsor, who replied14 that the investigator attempted to contact the patient after he missed a study visit, and was informed by his wife that he had died of a CVA. The sponsor stated they did not have any medical records in support of the CVA. I believe that the patient's wife likely provided accurate information on cause of death, but the fact that the CVA diagnosis was not verified should be taken into consideration.
L2260: ## 1.4 Deaths in the CD Indication
L2261: 4. **Patient 0602 (Botox-treated, Cervical Dystonia, Study 9801):** This 59-year-old female received two injections of Botox on April 9, 1999 and April 23, 1999, as per protocol. She was admitted to hospital on (b) (6) due to jaundice, passage of fresh blood per rectum, nausea, vomiting, and shortness of breath on exertion. She had experienced pale loose stools for three months, which had progressed to almost hourly bowel movements. Advanced, metastatic colon carcinoma, a large, hemorrhagic duodenal ulcer, and decompensated myocardial insufficiency were diagnosed. The subject developed hepatorenal failure and died (b) (6) of her malignant disease.
L2262: **Reviewer comment:** The diagnosis of advanced cancer within several months of entering the study does not suggest a causal relationship with NT 201 treatment.
L2263: ## 7.3.1.3 Comparison of Death Rates: Drug Versus Placebo
L2264: Due to the small number of deaths and the fact that all three NT 201-treated deaths occurred in the open-label portion of studies, a comparison in death rates between drug and placebo treatment groups could not be performed.
L2265: ## 7.3.1.4 Relationship of Death to Dose
L2266: **Reviewer comment:** The small number of deaths in Xeomin-treated patients (N=3), as well as overall (N=6 total deaths), precluded a formal analysis of deaths in relation to dose. Nevertheless, to thoroughly examine the deaths, the role of dose in the deaths should at least be considered. However, the relationship to dose is confounded by indication, as subjects in the spasticity indication both received higher doses and were older and more medically compromised than in other indications. I would therefore conclude that the data within the Xeomin development program neither supports nor refutes a dose-response relationship for NT 201 and fatal outcome.
L2267: 14 Response via e-mail, received May 18, 2010.
L2268: ## 7.3.1.5 Sponsor Conclusions with Reviewer Discussion
L2269: In their assessment of the deaths within the NT 201 development program, the sponsor reached the following conclusion:
L2270: “In NT 201-treated subjects, fatal events show a strong association with the subjects’ underlying state of health, and no pattern of increased incidence of fatal events with higher NT 201 dose or number of treatments is noted (ISS, pg. 40).”
L2271: **Reviewer comment:** *I generally agree with the sponsor's assessment that the deaths within the NT 201 studies do not show a pattern suggestive of a relationship with NT 201 treatment. I base this conclusion on the following:*
L2272: **Nature of the deaths:** *The three Xeomin-treated patients who died were older patients with risk factors associated with their cause of death. For example, Patient 1169 died from a severe CVA, and had entered the SP development program with a history of prior CVA. Clearly, some deaths from CVA and MI would be expected any study tracking older patients during the time period covered by the studies, and particularly when patients entered the study with a prior history of CVA, as was true for the SP indication.*
L2273: *A concern with previously approved Botulinum toxins has been adverse events stemming from systemic spread of toxin. None of the three NT 201-treated deaths were characteristic of a fatality from systemic spread of botulinum toxin. The possible exception is Patient 1054, who died in his sleep of unknown causes with no autopsy performed. Although this could conceivably represent an atypical presentation of systemic spread, this is unlikely due to the timing of the death (136 days after last NT 201 treatment) and the lack of escalating symptoms of systemic spread prior to death. The likelihood of this death being due to systemic spread is even lower considering that this patient had a CVA approximately nine days before his death*
L2274: **Timing of deaths:** *The three deaths in NT 201-treated patients occurred 21 days (presumed cerebrovascular accident), 44 days (“cardiac arrest”) and 136 days (unknown cause of death). The latter case has a latency that is outside of the expected length of NT 201 effect, but the other deaths took place during the time period in which NT 201 would have an effect.*

L2292: **Reviewer comment:** Of note, even when subjects from all indications are pooled, and when the adverse events are collapsed into SOC categories, there was frequently a single event in the NT 201 patients. The table above illustrates the difficulties in performing sub-analyses with regards to SAEs, as even when presented by SOC there are so few patients in each strata that examination by dose or risk modifiers is not possible.
L2293: ## 7.3.2.1.2 SAEs: Pooled Indications – Placebo-Controlled Studies
L2294: Merz reported that patients receiving NT 201 in the placebo-controlled studies had a slightly higher incidence of SAEs overall (17 SAEs/632 patients; 2.7%), compared with subjects who received placebo (5 SAEs, 346 patients; 1.4%)(120-Day Safety Update, pg. 41).
L2295: Merz performed a number of analyses to examine risk modifiers for SAEs. In the placebo-controlled studies, the sponsor found that male subjects who received NT 201 had a higher incidence of SAEs (5.9%), compared with the male subjects who received placebo (1.7%). However, because the number of patient in the demographic subgroups was small, it is difficult to draw reliable conclusions based on these data. No imbalance between treatment groups was seen for age or previous treatment status (120-Day Safety Update, pg. 43).
L2296: **Reviewer comment:** SAE data from open-label, multiple dose studies are presented within the sections for their specific indications below.

L2309: **Reviewer comment:** The SAE of dyspnea occurred in a 78-year-old man (Patient 2064) with a past medical history of vertigo, hypertension, peptic ulcer, partial thyroidectomy, cholecystectomy, melanoma of the eye, prostate resection, polypectomy and low hemoglobin
L2310: related to peptic ulcer. Concomitant medications included verapamil, terazosin, enalapril, simvastatin, omeprazole, amoxicillin, and moxifloxacin. During the course of the study, the patient was diagnosed with cardiomyopathy and congestive heart failure (ventricular ejection fraction 30%).
L2311: Four months after receiving 40 U of NT 201 in Study 0433/2, the patient was hospitalized for treatment of atrial fibrillation. He presented with complaints of chest pressure and shortness of breath. A CT scan showed cardiomegaly, and an echocardiogram revealed a left ventricular ejection fraction of 25%. He was treated with warfarin and amiodarone, and the event was reported to resolve (0433/2 Study Report, pg. 198).
L2312: The clinical scenario described is consistent with dyspnea as a result of congestive heart failure, and does not have any other characteristics, such as muscular paralysis, associated with systemic spread of botulinum toxin. In addition, the event occurred four months following the last dose with NT 201. I would therefore not consider this event to be a result of systemic spread of NT 201.
L2313: (Please see Section 7.3.2.5 of this review for comments on the occurrence of convulsion as an SAE in NT 201-treated patients.)
L2314: ## 7.2.3.2.4 SAEs in the Other BEB Studies
L2315: In BEB Study 0003, Merz stated that three of 148 subjects (2.0%) in the NT 201 group, and five of 152 subjects (3.3%) in the Botox® group, experienced SAEs. The three SAEs in the NT 201-treated patients were abdominal pain, enterocolitis and hemorrhagic intestinal diverticulum. No SAEs occurred in Study 0703.
L2316: **Reviewer comment:** After neurologic events, the second most common SOC for SAEs in all (placebo-controlled plus open-label) NT 201-treated patients are gastrointestinal disorders (See FDA Table 28 above). The narratives of the cases above did not specify if the AEs of abdominal pain and enterocolitis were related to constipation or diarrhea. Although it was the second most frequent SOC, in the placebo-controlled trials (for all indications) there was only 1 gastrointestinal SAE in a NT 201-treated patient, compared to none in placebo patients.
L2317: For the three gastrointestinal SAEs in Study 0003, the patient's ages were 46 years (abdominal pain), 72 years (enterocolitis) and 74 years (hemorrhagic diverticulum).

L2335: In the OLEX Period, the subject received NT 201 on December 5, 2007. About (b) (6) later, (b) (6) the patient underwent an appendectomy and removal of a benign colonic polyp. The patient received 3 units of packed red blood cells and transferred to the intensive care unit on a ventilator (0408/2 Final Study Report).

The patient under went a laproscopic cholecystectomy. In the second patient (Patient 1162), the pancreatitis was reported to have occurred in a patient with high alcohol consumption (0408/2 Final Study Report).

L2345: **Reviewer comment:** For all the CD studies, the SAEs which occurred in more than one patient in the CD indication were neoplasm (7 patients), myocardial infarction (three patients), transient ischemic attacks (TIA)/cerebrovascular accident (CVA) (3 patients), and pancreatitis (two patients). However, with the exception of pancreatitis, these are generally the most prevalent SAEs in any patient population, including in the general background population not treated with any botulinum toxin. Due to the small number of events, however, a formal comparison to background rates would not be a very robust analysis.

L2363: ## 7.3.2.5 SAEs: SP – Other Studies
L2364: Merz reported that total of 17 subjects in Studies (b) (4) 0607 (b) (4) experienced a total of 23 SAEs (SP ISS, pg. 31).
L2365: **Reviewer comments:** A tabular listing of all the SAEs within the NT 201 development program is contained in Appendix 9.5 of this review, including those from SP Studies (b) (4). I reviewed these SAEs, and as with the other NT 201 studies, the SAEs were typical of an older patient population with a history of a prior CVA (including myocardial infarction, CVA/TIA and pulmonary issues).
L2366: Of note, in the spasticity indication there were six cases of “epilepsy” and one case of “convulsion” among the SP SAEs. I reviewed the case reports for these patients:
L2367: - Patient 161 (b) (4) had a history of epilepsy for one year prior to study entry.
- Patient 1004 (Study 0607) (a 56-year-old man) had a single seizure 125 days after his last treatment with NT 201. He had recently discontinued a benzodiazepine derivative drug (clobazem) which likely contributed to the seizure.
- Patient 1116 (b) (4) experienced a single seizure 87 days after receiving 400 U of NT 201. He was hospitalized and evaluated but no likely trigger of the seizure was identified.
- Patient 1138 (Study 0607) had a history of “epileptic aura” for which he was being treated prior to entering the NT 201 study.
- Patient 1119 (Study 0410/1) On (b) (4) (31 days after administration of NT 201,) the patient experienced “severe status epilepticus” and moderate paraparesis for which he was hospitalized. The sponsor’s narrative refers to this event as status epilepticus, but states that it resolved after a 25 days, which is inconsistent with status epilepticus. The patient was treated with metoclopramide, valproate sodium, diazepam, piracetam, and mannitol. The subject’s epilepsy medication was also altered in the aftermath of these SAEs: “Status epilepticus” resolved on (b) (4). The patient was subsequently diagnosed with moderate epilepsy. He was hospitalized and topiramate (50 mg p.o./daily) was later added for this event.
- Patient 1029 (0410/2) had a history of epilepsy prior to study entry.
- Patient 0607 (0410/2) experienced a single seizure seven days after treatment with NT 201. He did not have a history of epilepsy prior to the study.
L2368: CVA can cause epilepsy, with the healed lesion acting as a nidus for seizure activity. As such, the analysis of drug-relatedness in the SP indication is confounded by this association. There were no cases of epilepsy in the other indication (BEB, CD) In addition, in many of the reports there was a pre-existing history of epilepsy/seizures or other contributing factors. I therefore consider the epilepsy/seizures as likely not related to NT 201 treatment.
L2369: ## 7.3.2.6 SAEs: SP – Anaphylaxis
L2370: After the BLA submission, there was a post-marketing report of anaphylaxis following Xeomin ® administration.
L2371: 1. Anaphylaxis Case reported to MedWatch (b) (4) # 09-0991): On August 24, 2009, a 56-year old British nurse (who was both the patient and reporter) was treated with NT 201 (Xeomin ®) 50 IU for glabellar line/wrinkle reduction. It was reported to be the subject's first treatment with Xeomin ®, although she had five prior treatments with Botox ®. Within three to five minutes after the injections, she experienced an anaphylactic reaction characterized by palpitations, faintness, and nausea lasting for 36 hours. The report stated hay fever, and allergic reactions to wasp stings and penicillin. The subject was not using any concomitant medication at the time of the event. In the opinion of the investigator, the event was medically significant and related to Xeomin ® administration. The report stated that there were no concomitant medications.
L2372: **Reviewer comment:** Although designated a case of anaphylaxis, the case above is unusual in that the no treatment was mentioned and the symptoms lasted for 36 hours.
L2373: **Reviewer comment:** I performed a literature search for case reports of anaphylaxis following botulinum toxin use. The search yielded only the single case below.
L2374: Li M, Goldberger BA, Hopkins C. Fatal case of Botox-related anaphylaxis? *J Forensic Sci* 2005; 50(1):169-72. This case report documents the first death associated with a Botox-lidocaine mixture given to a woman for chronic neck and back pain. Based on the medical records, autopsy, and laboratory findings, the cause of death was determined to be anaphylaxis to the Botox-lidocaine mixture. The history, indications, off-label uses and possible future applications of Botox are reviewed as well as the uses and complications of lidocaine. The author acknowledges that anaphylaxis cannot be definitively proven to be due to Botox alone.
L2375: **Reviewer comment:** Dr. Marc Stone's review of the Dysport ® BLA does not mention any cases of anaphylaxis. Botox labeling includes anaphylaxis and immediate hypersensitivity reactions in the postmarketing section and in Warnings and Precautions.
L2376: ## 7.3.2.7 SAEs: Reviewer Summary
L2377: The SAEs in the NT 201 development program were generally those expected for the background population of the same age group. There were two SAEs of respiratory failure and dyspnea, but neither fit the clinical presentation of systemic botulinum toxin spread and both cases had clear alternate causes (such as post-surgery blood loss). There were 6 cases of epilepsy and 1 case of convulsion among the SAEs, all within the spasticity indication. Some of these patients were known to have pre-existing epilepsy, but in others there was no history of epilepsy. CVA's may lead to subsequent seizures, and all of the patient sin the SP indication had experienced a prior CVA. There has been a post-marketing report of anaphylaxis occurring in a British patient.
L2378: ## 7.3.3 Dropouts and/or Discontinuations
L2379: ## 7.3.3.1 *Discontinuations: Pooled Indications*
L2380: Merz provided the following summary of discontinuations for all causes for the pooled NT 201 development program data.
L2381: **Reviewer comment:** To assess whether discontinuations due to adverse event were mistakenly classified into other discontinuation categories (withdrawal of consent, patient decision, investigator decision, etc.), I read the narratives for non-AE discontinuations within the NT 201 development program (ISS 120-Day Safety Update, Appendix D, pg, 180). Although some of the narratives lacked details, I was not able to find any instances in which a withdrawal due to AE had been miscoded.
L2382: In the pooled controlled studies, Merz stated that six of 1313 subjects (0.5%) in the NT 201 treatment group discontinued due to a TEAE, compared with 1 of 396 (0.3%) Botox-treated subjects and 1 of 346 (0.3%) placebo-treated subjects (ISS, pg 140).
L2383: **Reviewer comment:** There are a relatively small number of patients who discontinued the study due to adverse event (0.5%) given the number of patients treated (N=1313). For reference, this was comparable to development programs for other botulinum toxins. In the Dysport ® development program, there were relatively few withdrawals as well, as shown by the quote from Dr. Stone's15 safety review below:
L2384: There were only two adverse events associated with dropouts. In Study 020, a 52 year-old woman reported a number of complaints which started three days after injection, including headache, heavy eyelids as well as nervousness and irritability. All the events were recorded as lasting for approximately four weeks. In open-label Study 731, a 53 year-old woman experienced severe dysphagia six days after treatment, resolving 20 days later (pg. 12).
L2385: Merz reported that events leading to drop-out in the NT 201 treatment group included muscular weakness (two subjects), dizziness, neck pain, musculoskeletal pain, and nausea (one subject each). In the placebo patient, the event leading to drop-out was intracranial hematoma (ISS 120-Day Safety Update, pg. 139). The sponsor summarized all the adverse events leading to discontinuations within the NT 201 development program in the table below.

L2395: **Reviewer comment:** The sponsor commented that the number of subjects in the NT 201 treatment group who dropped out of the pooled, controlled studies because of a TEAE is too small (six subjects) to allow for meaningful analysis by age, gender, race, dose group, baseline disease severity or Botulinum neurotoxin pre-treatment status, with which I concur.
L2396: ## 7.3.3.2 Discontinuations: BEB

Of the subjects in the NT 201 group who dropped out, three developed or experienced withdrawal criteria, one was mistakenly given open-label study treatment, and one was randomized but never treated because she was found not to meet the study entry criteria. No subject in the Study 0433/1 Main Period withdrew due to a TEAE (BEB 120 Day ISS, pg. 24).

L2427: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the narrative for the spasticity patient who withdrew from 0410/2 due to dysphagia (Patient 1142), as this is not an expected AE within the spasticity indication. The event occurred in a 74-year-old man, and the narrative was lacking in detail,
L2428: stating only (b) (6), the subject developed the TEAE dysphagia. The event, which was mild in intensity, resolved on an unspecified date. The study drug was withdrawn as a consequence of this event." (ISS Appendix D, pg. 202).
L2429: **Reviewer comment:** A request was made to the sponsor for additional information on this case, including the timing of dysphagia with regard to NT 201 treatment. The sponsor responded with the following enhanced narrative:
L2430: **Subject 1142 (Adverse Event: DYSPHAGIA):** This 74-year-old male subject (height 176 cm, weight 68.5 kg, BMI 22.1) was an ex-smoker and had a past medical history significant for ischaemic cerebrovascular accident. At entry to the Main Period (baseline) it had been 1.3 years since the occurrence of the ischemic stroke and since the first diagnosis of upper limb spasticity. The subject was Botulinum toxin treatment-naïve. An ECG was performed at Screening and the results were abnormal and clinically relevant (Flat T-Wave in T2-T4 leads). The subject entered the Main Period on 03-Jul-2006 and received a first injection with 330 U of NT 201 on 10-Jul-2006, injected under EMG stimulator control for the treatment of the clinical patterns of Flexed Wrist, Clenched Fist, Flexed Elbow and Thumb in Palm. No adverse events were reported for the subject during the Main Period. At entry to the OLEX Period on 02-Oct-2007, the following concomitant diseases and medications were ongoing in this subject: hypertension (amlodipine 10 mg p.o./daily and enalapril 30 mg p.o./daily); hypercholesterolemia; hemiparesis; drug hypersensitivity; electrocardiogram Q wave abnormal; facial paresis; hypoglossal nerve paresis; and hyperreflexia. The subject was also receiving treatment with acetylsalicylic acid (75 mg p.o./daily) for cerebrovascular accident prophylaxis. On 02-Oct-2006 and 02-Jan-2007, the subject received 310 U and 325 U of NT 201, respectively, injected under EMG stimulator control for the treatment of the clinical patterns of Flexed Elbow, Flexed Wrist and Clenched Fist.
L2431: - On 11-Oct-2006 the subject experienced an episode of dysphagia that was assessed as mild in intensity and related to study treatment by the investigator. The event resolved on an unknown date. On 03-Jan-2007, one day after the last injection with NT 201, the subject developed a second TEAE dysphagia. The event, which was also mild in intensity, resolved on an unspecified date. The study drug was withdrawn as a consequence of this event. The last study visit was on 19-Jul-2007. The Investigator assessed the event as related to the study treatment, no further details regarding both episodes of dysphagia were reported.
L2432: **Reviewer comment:** Although the narrative above was somewhat unclear on this point, this event describes a SP patient, who received NT 201 injection in the upper extremity, who twice experienced dysphagia following NT 201 treatment (310 U and 320 U for the first and second adverse events, respectively). Further, the event appeared temporally related to NT 201 administration, occurring 9 days after treatment in the first event and one day after treatment in the second event. This case raises concern of systemic spread of toxin effect, and is also discussed in Section 7.3.5.1 (AESIs) of this review.
L2433: ## 7.3.3.4.2 SP Discontinuations: Other Studies
L2434: Merz stated that no patients in Study (b) (4) withdrew due to a TEAE. In Studies 0607 and (b) (4), a total of three NT 201-treated patients withdrew due to TEAE.
L2435: - Patient 1025 experienced cerebral haemorrhage and transient ischemic attack in Study 0607
- Patient 114 experienced carotid artery stenosis in Study (b) (4)
- Patient 101 in Study (b) (4) experienced a myocardial infarction (described in Section 7.3.1 of this review)
L2436: **Reviewer comment – Summary of Discontinuations:** Although the number of discontinuations due to AE is low in the NT 201 development program, it is consistent with that seen in other botulinum toxin development programs. The number of discontinuations due to adverse event was (0.5% (6/1313 patients) in the NT 201 treatment group, 0.3% (1/396 patients) Botox-treated subjects and 0.3% (1/346 patient) among placebo-treated patients. During the course of the safety review, I did not notice a pattern among the patients discontinuing due to adverse event. I audited the narratives of patients who discontinued for reasons other than adverse events (such as withdrawal of consent and loss to follow-up).
L2437: ## 7.3.4 Significant Adverse Events
L2438: Any adverse event that is included among the symptoms of spread of toxin effect were closely examined within this review. Primary among these were adverse events related to pulmonary difficulties and respiratory distress. The patients in the NT 201 development program who experienced dyspnea or respiratory failure are discussed in Section 7.3.2 (SAEs) and Section 7.4.1 (Common AEs) of this review.
L2439: During the review of the primary submission, a case report of a report was received describing anaphylaxis shortly after NT 201 use for glabellar frown lines. This case and other information related to the possibility of anaphylaxis is discussed in further detail in Section 9.6 of this review.
L2440: ## 7.3.5 Submission Specific Primary Safety Concerns
L2441: ## 7.3.5.1 Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESIs)
L2442: During the planning of the NT 201 development program, the FDA requested that Merz monitor studies for Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESIs). A list of AESIs was developed based on adverse events that had been observed for other botulinum toxin products.
L2443: During the planning of the NT 201 development program, the FDA requested that Merz monitor studies for Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESIs). This list of AESIs was developed based on adverse events that had been observed for other botulinum toxin products. The FDA provided the guidance below to the sponsor following a sponsor question on the analysis of AESIs:
L2444: "The pooling of datasets for the above analyses should be for each indication and across all indications. In addition to the analysis based on the list of Adverse Events of Special Interest in the briefing book, please perform another analysis based on the following terms: Botulism, muscular weakness, muscle paresis, paralysis, paralysis flaccid, areflexia, hyporeflexia, hypotonia, peripheral nerve palsy, peripheral paralysis, cranial nerve palsies multiple, paresis cranial nerve, cranial nerve paralysis, bulbar palsy, facial paresis, facial palsy, accommodation disorder, diplopia, eyelid function disorder, eyelid ptosis, pupillary reflex impaired, vision blurred, vocal cord paralysis, vocal cord paresis, speech disorder, dysphonia, dysarthria, dysphagia, aspiration, pneumonia aspiration, dyspnea, respiratory depression, respiratory failure, respiratory arrest, diaphragmatic paralysis, bradycardia, dry mouth, constipation, ileus paralytic, pelvic floor muscle weakness, and urinary retention." (FDA Minutes for Sponsor Meeting on December 12, 2008)
L2445: ## 7.3.5.1.1 AESIs: Pooled Indications
L2446: In the placebo-controlled studies of NT 201, Merz reported that the overall incidence of AESIs in the NT 201 treatment group was higher than in the placebo group.
L2447: At the PT level, the only AESI that occurred with a greater incidence in the NT 201 group, compared with the placebo group, with a difference of \ge 2\% was dysphagia (28 of 701 subjects, 4.0%, vs. 5 of 346 subjects, 1.5%)(ISS, pg. 72).
L2448: ## 7.3.5.1.2 AESIs: BEB
L2449: In the BEB Study 0433/1 Main Period, Merz noted that 32 of 74 subjects (43.2%) in the NT 201 group and 9 of 34 subjects (29.5%) in the placebo group experienced adverse events of special interest (AESIs). For subjects in the NT 201-treated group, the most common of these events were eyelid ptosis (14 subjects, 18.9%), dry mouth (12 subjects, 16.2%), vision blurred (4 subjects, 5.4%) and dyspnea (4 subjects, 5.4%). For subjects in the placebo-treated group, the most common AESIs were eyelid ptosis (3 subjects, 8.8%), vision blurred, dysphagia, and muscular weakness (each in 2 subjects, 5.9%). The sponsor stated that all other AESIs occurred in &lt;1% of subjects in the NT 20 treatment group (BEB ISS, pg. 39).
L2450: **Reviewer comment:** It is notable that just under 10% of the patients in the placebo group reported ptosis. One would expect this to be a paralytic effect of NT 201, but the occurrence in the placebo group suggests it may also be injection dependent or related to patient expectations.
L2451: **Reviewer comment:** See also Section 7.5.3.2.2 of this review for details of an analysis of AESI's by age group.
L2452: When the OLEX data from study 433/2 was combined with the data from Study 0433/1 Main, the results were similar to those for the Study 0433/1 Main Period. (BEB ISS, pg. 40).
L2453: ## 7.3.5.1.3 AESIs: CD
L2454: In the Main Period of Study 0408/1, the sponsor stated that the overall incidence of AESIs was higher in NT 201- treated subjects (37 [23.3%] of 159) than in placebo-treated subjects (3 [4.1%] of 74). In the pooled CD studies (Study 0408/1 Main Period, 9801, and 0013), Merz reported that 69 of 431 subjects (16.0%) in the NT 201 treatment group, and 3 of 74 subjects (4.1%) in the placebo group, experienced AESIs. For NT 201-treated subjects, the most common of these events were dysphagia (12.3%) and muscular weakness (4.2%). All other AESIs occurred in &lt;1% of subjects in the NT 201 treatment group (CD ISS, pg. 28).
L2455: Merz examined a number of risk modifiers for the association of AESIs in the CD indication. Merz stated that no difference in AESI incidence by age or gender was observed. However, the incidence of AESIs appears to decrease in NT 201-treated subjects with moderate to severe disease at baseline, compared with those with mild disease. Although the reason for this relationship was unclear, the sponsor hypothesized the following:
L2456: "One possible explanation is that subjects with moderate and severe CD often have substantial muscle hypertrophy due to the continuous muscle activity. Botulinum neurotoxin administration usually targets these hypertrophic muscles. These hypertrophic muscles present a substantial tissue mass that the Botulinum neurotoxin must diffuse across before reaching non-targeted muscles to cause AESIs. Conversely, in subjects with mild disease, the targeted muscles have relatively less tissue, and thereby present less of a barrier to diffusion. The proposed clinical administration of NT 201 includes flexible dosing to adjust for the mass of the targeted muscle (see Section 1.14.1.3, Package Insert, Section 2.4.1, Dosage). However, in the clinical trials, there was substantially less flexibility in the dosing; patients with mild, moderate, and severe disease received similar, protocol-specified doses of NT 201; because subjects with more severe disease most likely had more muscle mass, these subjects may have been less likely to experience AESIs."(CD ISS, pg. 30)
L2457: **Reviewer comment:** The sponsor's hypothesis above seems a reasonable explanation of the apparent inverse relationship between disease severity and the occurrence of AESIs.
L2458: In the Main Period of Study 0408/1, Merz stated that the incidence rates of AESIs with NT 201 treatment also "appears" higher in patients who were treatment-naïve at baseline and hypothesized that this was most likely due to enrollment bias (i.e., subjects who are pretreated and enroll in this study are preferentially subjects who had no adverse experience with previous Botulinum toxin administration)(CD ISS, pg. 57).
L2459: **Reviewer comment:** *The sponsor's hypothesis regarding the association of pre-treatment status and AESIs is plausible.*
L2460: ## 7.3.5.1.4 AESIs: SP
L2461: In the Study 0410/1 Main Period, Merz reported that 1 of 73 subjects (1.4%) in the NT 201 group (reported AE terms: constipation and paraparesis) and 1 of 75 subjects (1.3%) in the placebo group (reported AE term: dysphagia) experienced AESIs (SP ISS, pg. 43).
L2462: **Reviewer comment:** *The lack of a difference between AESIs in the placebo and treatment group is unexpected. In addition, it is inconsistent with the findings in the other indications: the risk of AESIs in the BEB indication was about twice as high in treated patients and about four times as high in the CD indication. However, in the pooled SP studies (described in the text immediately below) AESIs show a two-fold increase consistent with the other indications.*
L2463: In the pooled SP studies (the Study 0410/1 Main Period and Study 0607), Merz stated that 6 of 265 subjects (2.3%) in the NT 201 treatment group, and 1 of 75 subjects (1.3%) in the placebo group, experienced adverse events of special interest (AESIs). For NT 201-treated subjects, the most common of these events was muscular weakness (3 subjects, 1.1%). All other AESIs (i.e., dysphagia, constipation, dysarthria, and paraparesis) occurred in only one subject in the NT 201 treatment group (SP ISS, pg. 43).
L2464: In the Study 0410/2 OLEX Period, Merz reported that nine subjects treated with NT 201 experienced AESIs. The events experienced by these subjects were muscular weakness (5 subjects), hemiparesis (2 subjects), dysphagia (1 subject), and dry mouth (1 subject). Merz performed a number of sub-analyses and stated that the incidence of AESIs did not increase with greater number of NT 201 injections or longer observation period (SP ISS, pg. 44).
L2465: ## 7.3.5.2 Spread of Toxin Effect
L2466: Merz conducted two studies specifically to examine the diffusion potential of NT 201: Study 0709 CSR, and the Study 0113.
L2467: Merz asserted that, as shown in the descriptions of the most common adverse events, no symptoms indicative of clinically important systemic diffusion of NT 201 (e.g., general or respiratory muscle paresis, diplopia, dysarthria) were observed in NT 201 development program. The sponsor also stated that other potential indicators (e.g., non-specific muscular weakness, dyspnea, paresthesia, dysesthesia) occurred in a small number of subjects and the only adverse event suggestive of local diffusion that occurred more often in NT 201 patients, compared to placebo, with a difference of \ge 2\%, was dysphagia (5.0% vs. 1.5%) (Clinical Overview, pg. 32).
L2468: **Reviewer comment:** *In addition to the general review of AEs, I searched the indication specific (BEB, CD, SP) summaries for cases "dyspnea," "respiratory" and "paralysis" in an attempt to*
L2469: *capture all AEs that may be related to severe systemic spread of NT 201. This search revealed a number of cases of dyspnea and respiratory difficulty (pulmonary embolism, chronic bronchitis, asthma exacerbation, etc.), but none fit the pattern of systemic botulinum toxin spread. These cases are discussed in Section 7.3.2 (SAEs) and 7.4.1 (Common Adverse Events).*
L2470: One case that raised concern of systemic spread occurred in an SP patient (Patient 1142) who received NT 201 injection in the upper extremity, who twice experienced dysphagia following NT 201 treatment (310 U and 320 U for the first and second adverse events, respectively). Further, the event appeared temporally related to NT 201 administration, occurring nine days after treatment in the first event and one day after treatment in the second event.
L2471: **Reviewer Conclusion:** *I recommend that the Boxed Warning describing systemic spread of toxin effect contained in the label of other botulinum toxin class members also be included in the NT 201 labeling.*
L2472: ## 7.3.5.3 Hyperglycemia
L2473: ## 7.3.5.3.1 Background
L2474: During the review of another botulinum toxin product (Dysport ®) a small elevation of blood glucose was observed in drug-treated compared to placebo-treated patients. The Dysport ® label contains the following statement in the “Laboratory Findings” section:
L2475: “Subjects treated with DYSPORT ® exhibited a small increase from baseline (0.23 mol/L) in mean blood glucose relative to placebo-treated subjects. This was not clinically significant among subjects in the development program but could be a factor in patients whose diabetes is difficult to control.”
L2476: Due to this finding in the Dysport ® review, the FDA requested16 that the sponsor perform expanded analyses of glucose within the NT 201 development program.
L2477: ## 7.3.5.3.2 Hyperglycemia: Pooled Indications
L2478: At the request of FDA17, Merz performed additional analyses of the effects of treatment with NT 201 on blood glucose, by dose and duration, were performed for the pooled NT 201 safety database.
L2479: The sponsor stated that an analysis of mean changes from baseline to the primary endpoint assessment and final visits by NT 201 dose group “did not reveal a trend.” However, in the &gt;360 U NT 201 treatment group, Merz reported that the mean (SD) change in blood glucose level in
L2480: 16 Meeting minutes (December 12, 2008 Meeting)
L2481: 17 Request sent 2008
L2482: NT 201-treated patients from baseline to the primary endpoint assessment visit was 1.1 (1.38) mmol/L. Merz noted that this increase in glucose did not persist when the change from baseline to the final visit was analyzed 0.32 (1.56) mmol/L. The sponsor noted that the &gt;360 U NT 201 group patients were all from the SP studies. In the pooled SP studies, the sponsor reported that “a similar but more pronounced increase in blood glucose values occurred in the placebo group for the same time period.”
L2483: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the tables referenced by the sponsor18 and concurred with the sponsor's description summarized above. For the pooled data, the picture is mixed. Although there was a slight increase with NT 201 treatment to primary assessment, the effect did not persist to the final study visit, and a larger increase was also observed in the placebo group.
L2484: Merz asserted that there were no clinically meaningful difference in mean changes from baseline to the primary endpoint assessment and final visit between subjects followed for &gt;2 to ≤24 weeks, and those followed for &gt;24 to ≤48 weeks (ISS 120-Day Safety Update, pg. 89).
L2485: **Reviewer comment:** In the specific indications summarized in the sections below, and throughout their safety documents, Merz presented glucose data with the placebo-controlled and open-label data combined.
L2486: Table 37: Change in Glucose from Baseline to Final Study Visit in the Placebo-Controlled Phase of Study0433 (BEB), Study 0408 (CD) and Study 0410 (SP)

L2491: **Reviewer comment:** The table above shows that the mean glucose was usually higher at the final visit compared to baseline. However, this trend occurs in both the NT 201 and the placebo-treated patients, and is more pronounced in the placebo patients. However, as with the vital signs analysis (Section 7.4.3), the laboratory testing at the final visit was drawn weeks after the previous NT 201-treatment, and hence at a time when the effect of NT 201 had waned.
L2492: ## 7.3.5.3.3 Hyperglycemia: BEB
L2493: Merz presented serum glucose data as mean values and shifts from the previous injection within the BEB Study 0433/1 Main Period and Study 0433/2 OLEX Period (analyzed together), and asserted that “no trend toward an increase in mean serum glucose values over time, or increases in serum glucose in the groups of subjects with higher numbers of NT 201 injections, versus lower numbers of injections, was observed.” Further, Merz stated that there were no clinically meaningful differences in serum glucose values between the dose groups and no pattern of clinically meaningful shifts in serum glucose values from the previous study visit (ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 63).
L2494: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the tables the sponsor quoted (ISS Appendix K, 5.1.1.1, and 5.2.1.1) and verified that glucose levels over the course of the study and between the NT 201 and placebo treatments groups were essentially equivalent.
L2495: Two patients in the BEB Study 0433/1 Main Period and Study 0433/2 OLEX Period had high a “clinically significant” serum glucose value:
L2496: - **Patient 2011** was a 48-year-old man who received a single dose of NT 201 in the Main Period of the study, and five doses of NT 201 in the OLEX Period. The patient did not report a previous history of hyperglycemia or diabetes mellitus. The patient's serum blood glucose values at screening and before the second injection of study treatment were within normal limits (normal range: 68 to 110 mg/dL). Before the third injection, on April 23, 2007, the subject's serum glucose value was 140 mg/dL. The patient also had elevated glucose values of 160 mg/dL before the 5th injection, and 198 mg/dL at the final visit. The subject received a total of six injections, and completed the study.
L2497: **Reviewer comment:** The patient above (Patient 2011) is notable for an apparently escalating blood glucose with subsequent injections (Normal at baseline, 140 mg/dL before the third injection, 160 mg/dL before the fifth injection, and 198 mg/dL at final visit. Based on this single case, however, it is not possible to make a conclusion regarding causality.
L2498: 2. **Patient 2129** was a 68-year-old woman who received a single dose of NT 201 in the Main Period of the study, and one dose of NT 201 in the OLEX Period. She was noted to have hyperglycemia at screening for the Main Period of the study, with a serum glucose value of 126 mg/dL (normal range 68 to 110 mg/dL). She did not report a previous history of hyperglycemia or diabetes mellitus. The patient had elevated serum glucose values of 173 and 126 mg/dL before the 2nd injection and last observation visit respectively. The patient was withdrawn from the study because of a protocol violation (the subject was mistakenly given open-label NT 201, instead of randomized, double-blind treatment with NT 201 or placebo, at the first injection).
L2499: ## 7.5.3.3.4 Hyperglycemia: CD
L2500: Merz stated that in the Main Period of Study 0408/1, there were “no clinically important mean changes or shifts from baseline to the final visit in NT 201-treated patients.”
L2501: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the sponsor's tables displaying the glucose-related data in the placebo-controlled CD study 0408/1 (Table 6.4.1.2.1.2.2, Table 6.4.3.2.1.2.2, 0408/1 Study Report) and agreed that there were no apparent changes in mean glucose over time.
L2502: Merz also analyzed serum glucose mean values and shifts from the previous injection for the Study 0408/1 Main Period and Study 0408/2 Extension Period (analyzed together). The sponsor found no trend toward an increase in mean serum glucose values over time, or increases in serum glucose in the groups of subjects with higher numbers of NT 201 injections, versus lower numbers of injections. For the 153 subjects who completed the Study 0408/2 Extension Period as of October 2008, and have unblinded NT 201 dose data, the sponsor stated that there was no clinically meaningful difference in mean serum glucose values between the \le 120 U group and the &gt;120 to 240 U group, or from prior study visit (ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 62).
L2503: In the Study 0408/1 Main Period and Study 0408/2 Extension Period, a total of seven subjects experienced CS abnormal serum glucose values. However, for five of these patients the value was a baseline test prior to NT 201 treatment. The two treatment-emergent cases are shown below (ISS 120 Day Update, pg. 63):
L2504: 1. **Patient 1107** had a CS high serum glucose value of 7.5 mmol/L (normal range 3.8 to 6.1 mmol/L) before injection 4. The subject's serum glucose value before the 5th injection was within normal limits. This subject received NT 201 120 U in the Main Period, and 240 U in the Extension Period. She had elevated hepatic alkaline phosphatase, total cholesterol, and LDL cholesterol values, and a normal serum glucose value, at screening
L2505: for the Main Period. Her past medical history did not include diabetes or glucose intolerance, and she did not report medications for the treatment of these conditions during the study. The subject's TEAEs were musculoskeletal pain, neck pain, headache, pain in extremity, and joint swelling.
L2506: 2. **Patient 1049** had a CS low serum glucose (2.9 mmol/Labeling) at the last study visit. She had received six injections and had no other CS abnormalities in serum glucose values during the study.
L2507: ## 7.5.3.3.5 Hyperglycemia: SP
L2508: Within the SP indication, the sponsor stated Patient 1009 in the Study 0410/2 OLEX Period experienced a SAE classified as "diabetes mellitus inadequate control." She had a history of diabetes as well as elevated glucose prior to the first injection, (137 mg/dL). At Visit 8, the patient's glucose value of 432 mg/dL, and she was hospitalized for control of her diabetes mellitus. The patient's medications were adjusted and the event resolved. At Visit 10, following the resolution of the event, the subject had a low, not CS glucose value of 57 mg/dL. Merz stated that the subject's remaining on-study glucose values were normal (97 mg/dL at Visit 12), or high but not clinically significant (CS) by the investigator (115 mg/dL at Visit 14 and 174 mg/dL at Visit 18). The sponsor stated that no action was taken with the study drug (ISS 120 Day Update, pg. 63).
L2509: **Reviewer comment:** Although this patient's pre-existing diabetes could explain the glucose levels noted above, it is also possible that treatment with NT 201 exacerbated her already high glucose levels (137 mg/dL) to an even higher level (432 mg/dL).
L2510: In the Study 0410/1 Main Period and Study 0410/2 OLEX Period (analyzed together), Merz reported that six patients who had serum glucose values at the first injection visit that were within normal limits experienced abnormalities in serum glucose values while receiving NT 201 during the study. However, five of the patients had a pre-existing history of diabetes. Study
L2511: Appears this way on original
L2512: **Reviewer comment – Overall Reviewer Conclusions:** The data linking NT 201 to an effect on glucose levels is mixed. There were cases of elevated blood glucose in NT 201-treated patients, including one in the spasticity indication that was classified as an SAE due to the need for hospitalization. Within the placebo-controlled trials, there were also more clinically significant glucose values in the NT 201 compared to the placebo treated patients, but the overall number of patients with events in each group was small. (For example, in the spasticity indication, 3 cases in the NT 201 group compared to none in the placebo group). When mean glucose value was compared from the initial study visit to the primary endpoint visit and the final visit with the placebo-controlled trials, the values for NT 201, placebo and Botox® were similar (both over time and between groups).
L2513: ## 7.3.5.4 Alkaline Phosphatase
L2514: ## 7.3.5.4.1 Alkaline Phosphatase: Pooled Indications
L2515: Because of a slight elevation of alkaline phosphatase in the safety review of a different botulinum toxin product, alkaline phosphatase has been evaluated in recent submissions of botulinum toxin products. In prior botulinum toxin products, the safety reviews there was a concern of elevated alkaline phosphatase levels, although the safety data was not sufficient to include any statement on the subject in either the Dysport ® or the Botox ® label.
L2516: **Reviewer comment:** As with the glucose analysis, the sponsor performed a variety of analyses to assess alkaline phosphatase levels in the NT 201 development program, including by indication, injection number, dose groups and over time. These were performed on the placebo-controlled and open-label data combined. The analyses typically involved shift changes and trends in alkaline phosphatase levels in the NT 201 treated group by various parameters. Because these tables were difficult to access and interpret, the sponsor was asked to complete the table below.

L2522: ## 7.3.5.4.2 Alkaline Phosphatase: BEB
L2523: Merz stated that an analysis of alkaline phosphatase mean values and shifts from the previous injection was performed for the Study 0433 (BEB) Main and OLEX Periods (analyzed together). The sponsor maintained that there were also no clinically meaningful differences in mean alkaline phosphatase values between the NT 201 dose groups” (Table 5.1.1.2, ISS Appendix K)(ISS, pg. 60).
L2524: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the table referenced by the sponsor for the statements in the paragraph above (Table 5.1.1.2, ISS Appendix K) and found them to be accurate.
L2525: Merz stated that two subjects had elevated alkaline phosphatase values, but that only one of these was treatment-emergent (the other was present before the patient received their first injections). The sponsor reported that Subject 2106 had a high, CS abnormal alkaline phosphatase value before Injection 2 (169 U/L; normal range 36-118 U/L). The sponsor noted, however, that this patient also had a high alkaline phosphatase value (135 U/L) before the first injection (ISS 120 Day Update, pg. 60).
L2526: ## 7.3.5.4.3 Alkaline Phosphatase: CD
L2527: Merz performed an analysis of alkaline phosphatase mean values and shifts from the previous injection for NT 201-treated patients in the Study 0408/1 Main Period or 0408/2 Extension Period (analyzed together) and found “no trend toward an increase in alkaline phosphatase over time, or of higher mean values in the groups of subjects with higher numbers of NT 201 injections, compared with those with lower numbers of injections” was observed. For the 166 subjects whose treatment assignment was unblinded, Merz reported that there was no clinically meaningful difference in mean alkaline phosphatase values or shifts from the previous injection, between the \le 120 U group and the &gt;120 to 240 U group (CD ISS, pg. 70).
L2528: Merz stated that in the open-label extension period (0408/2), two NT 201-treated patients had elevated alkaline phosphatase levels (CD ISS, pg. 60):
L2529: - **Subject 1107:** This 57-year-old woman, had an elevated alkaline phosphatase value of 140 U at screening (normal range 36-118 U), and elevated values of 132 U, 127 U and 136 U at the final visit of the Main Period, the 4th injection visit, and the 5th injection visit, respectively. At the trial termination visit the value was slightly elevated (122 U). The patient received one injection of NT 201 (120 U) during the Main Period, and four additional injections of NT 201 (240 U) during the Extension Period, and completed the study.
- **Subject 1316:** This a 72-year-old woman, had alkaline phosphatase values that were within normal limits (normal range 36-118 U) at all study visits until the final visit of the Extension Period, when her alkaline phosphatase value was 134 U. During the study, the patient also had multiple elevated gamma-glutamyltransferase values: 53 U/L (normal range 8 to 49 U/L) at screening; 53 U/L before the 3rd injection, and 70 U/L before the 4th injection, and 142 U/L at the final visit. The patient received NT 201 (240 U) in the Main Period and three injections of NT 201 (still blinded) during the Extension Period, and completed the study.
L2530: **Reviewer comment:** Because these elevations occurred in the OLEX study 0408/2, it is difficult to tell if they represent an elevated risk in the drug-treated patients compared to non-treated patients.
L2531: ## 7.3.5.4.4 Alkaline Phosphatase: SP
L2532: In the Study 0410 (SP) Main and OLEX Periods (analyzed together), Merz stated that there were no clinically meaningful mean changes from baseline or the previous injection, or shifts from baseline or the previous injection, for alkaline phosphatase values. One NT 201- treated patient who did not have a CS abnormality in alkaline phosphatase at study entry developed a CS abnormality during the study. Merz provided the following summary narrative (ISS 120 Day Update, pg. 61).
L2533: **Subject 1132** received placebo in the Main Period of Study 0410/1. The patient had a high, not CS alkaline phosphatase value of 136 U/L (normal range 35 to 123 U/L) before the first NT 201 injection, followed by normal values (115 U/L and 120 U/L at the primary endpoint analysis visit 4 weeks after injection, and before the second injection, respectively), and a CS high value of 172 U/L before the 4th injection. The patient's remaining values during the study were on the high end of the normal range (121 U/L at Visit 14 and 117 U/L at Visit 16).
L2534: **Reviewer comment:** There was no additional information following the patient's completion of the study to determine whether the alkaline phosphatase level returned to normal.
L2535: ## 7.3.5.4.5 Alkaline Phosphatase: Non-Neurologic Indications
L2536: Merz noted that one patient in the glabellar frown line studies had a CS alkaline phosphatases value following treatment with NT 201, as presented below:
L2537: **Subject 2033** is a 56-year-old white woman who was initially enrolled in Study 0520. The subject entered Study 0609 and received the first treatment with NT 201 in that study on October 5, 2007. She received a total of 4 cycles of treatment in Study 0609. Her medical history was significant for diverticulosis, sigmoidectomy, diabetes mellitus, and hypothyroidism. Concomitant medications included levothyroxine. At screening, on September 7, 2007, the patient's alkaline phosphatase value was 82 U/L (normal range 20 to 125 U/L). On October 6, 2008, at the 12-month evaluation visit, the subject experienced a CS high alkaline phosphatase value of 171 U/L. She experienced two AEs during the studies: bile duct stone which required hospitalization and moderate gastritis (ISS 130 Day Safety Update, pg. 37).
L2538: ## 7.4 Supportive Safety Results
L2539: As discussed in Section 7.3.5.2 of this review, the sponsor conducted two studies to examine systemic spread of NT 201 by measuring concentrations at different body sites.
L2540: ## 7.4.1 Common Adverse Events
L2541: **Reviewer comment:** *A phenomena has been observed in other drug development programs in which the risk of adverse events in studies conducted in North America are higher than those in studies conducted outside North America. For some drugs, the difference was limited to common adverse events, but in others a difference was observed for both common and serious adverse events. A comparison of AE risk in NT 201 studies conducted inside and outside of North American is contained in Section 7.5 of this review ("Other Safety Explorations").*
L2542: ## 7.4.1.1 Definition of Common Adverse Events
L2543: For the sponsor's analysis within the ISS, Merz utilized the following definitions:
L2544: - **Common adverse events** were defined as those occurring in \ge 1\% of subjects.
- **Common Treatment Emergent Adverse Events (TEAEs)** were defined as those with a difference of \ge 3\% to placebo at the SOC level and a difference \ge 2\% to placebo at the PT level.
- **Very common adverse events** were defined as those occurring in \ge 10\% of subjects
L2545: **Reviewer comment:** *In their analyses, Merz typically presents the chance of adverse events in the form of risk (percent), rather than rate (events per time). In the placebo-controlled Main period of the studies one would expect exposures (dose, number of injections, follow-up time) to be relatively equivalent between groups due to similar discontinuation rates (0.5% NT 201, 0.3% placebo). However, some analyses pooled placebo-controlled and open-label data, and in these analyses the NT 201-treated patients have spent more time in the study than placebo patients. This additional time provides more opportunity for NT 201 patient to experience an adverse*
L2546: *event, whether related to NT 201 or not. This presentation is acceptable, however, as this is a conservative method of analysis that is biased against the drug.*
L2547: ## **7.4.1.2 Common Adverse Events: Pooled Indications**
L2548: **Reviewer comment:** *Explorations for dose-response and the presence of possible risk modifiers for common AEs are contained in Sections 7.5.1 (Drug-Dose Interaction) and 7.5.2 (Drug-Demographic Indication) respectively.*
L2549: The following table presents common (\ge 1\%) and very common (\ge 10\%) adverse events in the pooled placebo-controlled studies within the total NT 201 development program (Clinical Overview, pg. 27).

L2560: **Reviewer comment:** A table of common adverse event is particularly poorly suited to pooling indications because the AEs are indication specific. For example, dysphagia is a common AE in NT 201-treated CD patients, but not in other indications. Likewise for ptosis, which is a common AE for BEB but not for the other indications. Combining the indications dilutes AEs that are indication specific and therefore biases the results.
L2561: Keeping this limitation in mind, in the pooled placebo-controlled trials the AEs with the largest difference between NT 201 and patients were dysphagia (4.0% NT 201 vs. 1.5% placebo), neck pain (2.9% NT 201 vs. 0.9% placebo), muscular weakness (2.1% NT 201 vs. 0.9% placebo), musculoskeletal pain (1.6% vs. 0.3%), dry mouth (2.0% versus 0.3%), and ptosis 2.3% vs. 0.9%). These AEs are expected within the botulinum toxin class. There was also an increase in NT 201 risk relative to placebo for diarrhea (1.4% NT 201 vs. 0.9% placebo) and nausea (1.4% NT 201 versus 0.3% placebo), which are not well known to be associated with botulinum toxin products (Diarrhea is mentioned in the post-marketing events in the Botox® label and Dysport® label).
L2562: Merz acknowledged that in the pooled, placebo-controlled studies, a higher percent of subjects in the NT 201 treatment group experienced TEAEs, compared with the placebo group. The SOCs in which a greater percent of subjects in the NT 201 treatment group compared with the placebo group experienced TEAEs, with a difference of \ge 3\%, were Nervous system disorders, Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders, Gastrointestinal disorders, Eye disorders, and Respiratory, thoracic and mediastinal disorders. The TEAEs that occurred in a greater percent of subjects in the NT 201 group, compared with the placebo group, with a difference of \ge 2\% were headache and dysphagia (ISS, pg. 46).

L2598: ## 7.4.1.3.3 Common Adverse Events: BEB – Open-Label Studies
L2599: **Reviewer comment:** For the BEB indication, the sponsor presented the open-label AE data combined with the AE data from the placebo-controlled portion of the study.
L2600: Merz stated that the most common AEs in the repeated dose BEB studies were eyelid ptosis (18.9%), dry eye (16.2%), dry mouth (16.2%), and visual disturbance (6.8%)(BEB ISS, pg. 26).
L2601: ## 7.4.1.4 *Common Adverse Events: CD*
L2602: ## 7.4.1.3.1 *Common Adverse Events: CD – Pooled Studies*
L2603: In pooled Main and Extension data from CD Study 0408/1, Merz stated that 194 of 227 subjects (85.5%) experienced TEAEs. In the Study 0410/1 Main Period and 0410/2 OLEX Period combined, the sponsor reported that TEAEs most frequently occurred in the nervous system disorders (30 of 147 subjects, 20.4%), infections (29 of 147 subjects, 19.7%), Investigations, and musculoskeletal/connective tissue disorders (14 subjects, 9.5%, each) SOCs. At the PT level, the sponsor reported that the common AE profile was similar to that in the placebo-controlled trial, and including muscle spasticity (15 subjects, 10.2%), upper respiratory tract infection, hypertension, epilepsy, and depression (7 subjects, or 4.8%, each) as the most frequent AEs (CD ISS, pg. 37).
L2604: **Reviewer comment:** *Two patients within the CD indication experienced common adverse events classified as dyspnea. Narratives for these two patients are shown below:*
L2605: - **Patient 5537:** This 51-year-old female subject (white, weight 119 lb, height 64 in) was an ex-smoker. Her medical history was significant for surgery to right scapula post MVA (motor vehicle accident) in 1981, concomitant diseases included constipation since 1971, panic/anxiety disorder since 1971, and intermittent left calf swelling since 1996. Concomitant medications were Correctol 2 tabs prn for constipation and Xanax 1 mg prn for anxiety, as well as several vitamin and mineral supplements. During the trial the generalized headache was treated with Aspirin 2-4tabl prn.
L2606: Torticollis was first diagnosed in March 2006. Estimated Duration of CD was 10 years. Before entering the study the subject had not been treated with any botulinum toxin. In the MP, the subject received 240 U of NT 201 on 25-OCT-2006. On 28-OCT-2006 the subject experienced stiff neck, which was rated as severe in intensity and related to treatment by the investigator. This event resolved after three days. On 08-NOV-2006 the subject suffered from mild dyspnea which was considered to be related to treatment and resolved after nine days. An investigator comment described this event as “two episodes when stair climbing”. Other non-serious adverse events that occurred on the same day were generalized headache (moderate), head tremors (mild), mumbled speech (mild, reported were intermittent episodes 4-5 times), neck weakness (severe), and left arm weakness (moderate). All of these events were considered treatment-related and for all events the outcome was reported as resolved. The subject continued in the extension period.
L2607: - **Patient 5047:** This 19-year-old male subject (white, weight 210.40 lb, height 70 in) was a non-smoker. His medical history was significant for enlarged heart in 2001 [sic] and pneumonia in 2006. His concomitant diseases included allergic rhinitis since 2005 and restless legs since 24-MAY-2007. At study entry, his concomitant medications comprised Requip 1 mg/day(25-MAY-2007 - 11- SEP-2007) for restless legs. During the main period,
L2608: the subject took Advil every 4 hrs pm, doxycycline 100 mg bid, Robitussin DM 2 tbsp 6 times a day, and Tylenol, all for bronchial infection (13-SEP-2007 - 30-SEP-2007), and Advair and Singulair (21-OCT-2007 - ongoing) for Asthma. Torticollis was first diagnosed in October 2005. Prior to study entry the subject had been treated with Botox® between 08-DEC-2005 and 24-MAY-2007. In the MP, the subject received 120 U of NT 201 on 30AUG-2007. On 02-SEP-2007 the subject experienced pain in bilateral shoulders, which was rated as moderate in intensity and related to treatment by the investigator. This event was ongoing but stable at the end of the trial. On the same day he experienced moderate weakness in bilateral shoulders, which was also considered to be treatment-related and resolved after two weeks. On (b) (6) the subject suffered an asthma attack which was moderate in intensity and not considered treatment-related by the investigator. (b) (6) later, on 31-OCT-2007, the subject experienced chest pain due to bronchial infection and shortness of breath. Both events were of mild intensity and assessed as not related to study treatment. Shortness of breath was reported as resolved 16 days after event onset, chest pain due to bronchial infection was ongoing but stable at the end of the trial. The subject continued in the extension period.
L2609: **Reviewer comment:** *Neither of the two cases above are strongly suggestive of dyspnea due to spread of toxin effect, as the first appears to describe exertional dyspnea and in the second patient the event was characterized as an asthma attack/bronchial infection.*

L2619: **Reviewer comment:** In the table above, the most frequent adverse event occurring more frequently with NT 201 treatment compared to placebo are those expected with botulinum toxin use in the cervical region: neck pain (10.7% NT 201 vs. 4.1% placebo), muscular weakness 14 (8.8% NT 201 vs. 1.4% placebo), musculoskeletal pain 8 (5.0% vs. 1.4%) and musculoskeletal stiffness (3.1% NT 201 vs. 1.4%)(CD ISS, pg. 36).
L2620: As a group, there were more “Respiratory, Thoracic and Mediastinal Disorders” in the NT 201-treated (11.3%) compared to placebo patients (2.7%). The AEs in this category included asthma,
L2621: *rhinorrhea and cough, which one would expect to be common in the background population. I reviewed the two cases of dyspnea.*
L2622: ## 7.4.1.3.3 Common Adverse Events: CD – Open-Label Studies
L2623: As of December 2008, Merz stated that a total of 177 of 214 subjects (82.7%) had experienced TEAEs in the ongoing Study 0408/2 Extension Period. Merz asserted that the most commonly experienced TEAEs in the Extension Period were similar to those in the placebo-controlled Main Period, and included dysphagia (34 of 214 subjects, 15.9%), sinus infection (16 of 214 subjects, 7.5%), “common cold” (13 of 214 subjects, 6.1%), headache and neck weakness (9 of 214 subjects, 4.2%, each)(CD ISS, pg. 29).

At the PT level, the event with the highest incidence in the NT 201 group was epilepsy (8 of 265 subjects, 3%). The sponsor stated that most TEAEs occurred in only one patient.

L2636: ## 7.4.1.4.2 Common Adverse Events: Spasticity – Placebo-Controlled Trials
L2637: In the Study 0410/1 Main Period, Merz stated in the NT 201 treatment group, the MedDRA System Organ Classes (SOCs) in which TEAEs most commonly occurred were Metabolism and nutrition disorders (6 of 73 subjects, 8.2%), Nervous system disorders, and Injury, poisoning and procedural complications (5 of 73 subjects, 6.9%, each). At the Preferred Term (PT) level, the most common event in NT 201-treated patients was hyperglycemia (3 of 73 subjects, 4.1%)(SP ISS, pg. 35).
L2638: **Reviewer comment:** *Hyperglycemia has been observed with use of other botulinum toxin products, specifically in the Dysport® development program. Hyperglycemia and glucose (including the three cases above) are discussed in more detail in Section 7.6.2.4 of this review.*

L2650: **Reviewer comment:** In the 0410 study report, common AEs were presented as those occurring in more than 2% of patients, as opposed to in 1% of patients in the majority of the NT 201 development program. This is acceptable, however, as it is still an inclusive percent (as opposed to 5%) and reduces some of the “noise” with use of a 1% cut-off.
L2651: In reviewing the table above, the AEs in the NT 201-treated group with the largest increase compare to placebo-treated patients were: headache (2.7% NT 201 [N=2] versus 1.3% placebo [N=1]), epilepsy (2.7% NT 201 versus 1.3% placebo) and hyperglycemia (4.1% NT 201 versus 0 placebo). I reviewed the two cases of epilepsy and both patients had a history of epilepsy prior to entering the study.
L2652: Hyperglycemia is discussed in Section 7.6.2.4 of this review.
