L2659: **Reviewer comment:** The table above is notable for several reasons. First, although as per the title it presents open-label data, it also presents figures for the placebo group, which were presumably taken from the Main Period (0410/1) of the study. Secondly, the table presents events occurring in \ge 2\% of patients, whereas in the majority of the NT 201 development program 1% was used as the criteria for common adverse events. In reviewing the table above, the most frequent adverse events in OLEX Study 0410/2 were: muscle spasticity (8.5%), depression (7.0%), upper respiratory track infection (5.6%) and muscular weakness (4.2%).
L2660: The events of hyperglycemia and increased GGT noted in the table above are discussed in Section 7.6.2.4 (Hyperglycemia) and Section 7.4.2 (Laboratory Findings) of this review.
L2661: ## 7.4.2 Laboratory Findings
L2662: **Reviewer comment:** Laboratory results for glucose and alkaline phosphatase are discussed in Section 7.6.2.4 and Section 7.6.2.5 of this review, respectively.
L2663: ## 7.4.2.1 Laboratory Values: Pooled Indications
L2664: Merz stated that in their safety analysis, laboratory values were summarized as shifts from baseline, and as mean values and changes from baseline, to the visit when the primary efficacy endpoint was assessed (this varied across studies, from 2 to Week 6 following study drug administration) and to the final visit (this also varied across studies, from 8 to 25 weeks following study drug administration)(ISS, pg. 79).
L2665: In the pooled controlled studies, Merz summarized that 41 of 1313 NT 201-treated subjects (3.1%) and 14 of 346 subjects (4.0%) in the placebo group, had treatment-emergent, clinically significant (CS) abnormalities of biochemistry parameters. In the NT 201 treatment group, these events were elevated glucose (11 subjects, 0.8%), elevated total cholesterol (9 subjects, 0.7%), elevated LDL cholesterol (9 subjects, 0.7%), elevated gamma-glutamyltransferase (8 subjects, 0.6%), elevated AST (6 subjects, 0.5%), low HDL cholesterol (4 subjects, 0.3%), elevated
L2666: alanine aminotransferase (4 subjects, 0.3%), elevated urea (4 subjects, 0.3%), elevated bilirubin (3 subjects, 0.2%), low potassium (2 subjects, 0.2%), low sodium, elevated alkaline phosphatase, and elevated creatinine (1 subject, 0.1%, each) (Table 6.3.1)(ISS 120-Day Safety Update, pg. 89).
L2667: **Reviewer comment:** *I reviewed the sponsor's values for the various biochemistry parameters to be characterized as clinically significant, and found them to be reasonable.*

L2692: For hematology studies, Merz noted that in the pooled, controlled studies, fewer patients in the NT 201 group than in the placebo group experienced clinically significant laboratory abnormalities; seven of 1313 NT 201-treated subjects (0.5%) had 10 treatment-emergent, clinically significant (CS) hematology abnormalities, compared with 4 of 346 subjects (1.2%) in the placebo group. The abnormalities that occurred in the NT 201-treated subjects were low hemoglobin (3 subjects, 0.2%), low erythrocytes (2 subjects, 0.2%), low platelets (2 subjects, 0.2%), low hematocrit, low relative reticulocytes, and elevated eosinophil count (1 subject, 0.1%, each). The sponsor stated that all of these abnormalities were detected at the final visit of the respective study, which in every study occurred weeks after receipt of a single dose of NT 201 (ISS, pg. 80).
L2693: For hematology results, in the pooled subjects in the NT 201 treatment group, in the controlled studies, Merz stated that there were no “clinically meaningful shifts or mean changes from baseline” in “most” biochemistry parameters. These include: albumin, alkaline phosphatase, bilirubin, calcium, high density lipoprotein (HDL) cholesterol, low density lipoprotein (LDL) cholesterol, total cholesterol, creatine kinase, creatinine, gamma-glutamyltransferase (GGT), potassium, aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), sodium and urea (ISS, pg. 80).

L2725: ## 7.4.3 Vital Signs
L2726: ## 7.4.3.1 *Pooled Indications*
L2727: **Reviewer comment:** In the Dysport® development program, a small effect on heart rate was observed, and the Dysport® label contains the following language within the "Electrocardiographic Findings" section of labeling:
L2728: ECG measurements were only recorded in a limited number of subjects in an open-label study without a placebo or active control. This study showed a statistically significant reduction in heart rate compared to baseline, averaging about three beats per minute, observed 30 minutes after injection.
L2729: **Reviewer comment:** Although one indication (BEB) showed a slight decrease in heart rate from baseline to final visits, this was not replicated in the other indications (CD and SP). The potential signal for decreased heart rate observed in the Dysport® development program does not appear to be replicated in the NT 201 development program.
L2730: This reviewer made a request to the sponsor regarding the timing of vital sign measurements with regard to NT 201 administration, as this was not readily apparent in the application. The sponsor's response to this information request is shown below (Sequence 40 within the NT 201 BLA):
L2731: "In the placebo-controlled studies for CD (i.e. study 0408), BEB (i.e. study 0433) and SP (i.e. study 0410) according to the trial protocol the vitals signs measurement at baseline were to be performed before randomization and therefore before injection of study medication (see also initial BLA submission, for CD: section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0408, 16.1.1 Amended Clinical Trial Protocol No. 2, section 9.5.1.4.3 and 9.5.1.4.4; for BEB: section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0433-1, 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments, section 9.5.1.4.3 and 9.5.1.4.4; for SP: section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0410-1 (amended report), 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments, section 9.5.1.4). In these studies at the injection visits in the extension period all assessments including vital signs were to be performed prior to treatment.
L2732: In SP study 0607 the vital signs measurement were not necessarily to be performed before injection according to the trial protocol (see also initial BLA submission, section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0607, 16.1 Study Information, section 9.1.6).
L2733: In the active comparator controlled studies for CD (i.e. study 0013) and BEB (i.e. study 0003) the assessments were expected to be performed in order of appearance and therefore vital signs assessments should have been performed before treatment (see also initial BLA submission, for CD: section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0013, 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments, section 10.2.1; for BEB: section 5.3.5.1 MRZ 60201-0003, 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments 3, section 10.2.1). In the active comparator CD study 9801 vital signs were assessed at baseline, no further specification regarding the time point relative to the injection was defined in the trial protocol.”
L2734: **Reviewer comment:** Based upon the above, in the placebo-controlled studies vital sign measurements were apparently made prior to treatment, both for baseline and for follow-up visits. As such, the vital signs were measured at least twelve weeks subsequent to prior NT 201 administration, at a time when NT 201's effect had waned. Clearly, this was not the ideal timing for assessing an effect of NT 201 on vital signs.
L2735: In the remaining studies (SP 0607, active-comparator studies), the vital signs were also performed prior to NT 201 injection, with the exception of CD Study 9801 in which (after baseline assessment) “no further specification regarding the time point relative to the injection was defined in the trial protocol.”
L2736: I referenced the FDA safety review19 of Dysport® to ascertain the timing of vital sign studies within the Dysport® development program, which was 30 minutes after injection.
L2737: ## 7.4.3.2 Vital Signs: BEB
L2738: Merz stated that in the pooled BEB studies, in the NT 201 treatment group, no clinically meaningful changes or shifts in systolic or diastolic blood pressure, heart rate or respiratory rate from baseline to the visit when the primary efficacy endpoint was measured (Week 2 to Week 4) or the final visit were noted. The sponsor further stated that in the unpoold Study 0703, no clinically meaningful changes in these parameters were observed (BEB ISS, 120-Day Update, pg. 55).
L2739: **Reviewer comment:** I examined BEB placebo-controlled study 0433/1 in order to:
L2740: - Compare NT 201 and placebo values for vital sign parameters
- Review the criteria the sponsor used to determine clinically meaningful changes
L2741: I searched the ISS and the Study 0433/1 study report for the parameters used to determine a “clinically meaningful shift” but did not find this information. A request for clarification was made to the sponsor, who provided the following information:
L2742: "A clinical meaningful shift (too low or too high) is defined as a vital sign that was out of the normal range for the respective parameter after administration. This definition applies for all studies analyzed for the Integrated Summary of Safety.
L2743: For example: If a subject has a heart rate as defined in the normal range of 50-120 beats per minutes at screening and develops a tachycardia (per definition a heart rate of &gt; 120 beats per minutes) during the course of the study, this subject would be counted as one subject with a clinical meaningful shift to the group of subjects with a high heart rate at the respective visit (Merz response to FDA question, transmitted via e-mail on January 19, 2010).

L2765: ## 7.4.3.4 *Vital Signs: SP*
L2766: In the pooled SP studies, Merz stated that in the NT 201 treatment group, “no clinically meaningful shifts or mean changes in systolic or diastolic blood pressure, heart rate or respiratory rate” from baseline to Week 4 or the final visit (which varied across studies, from 20 to 69 weeks following treatment with NT 201) were noted. For heart rate, in the pooled studies, the heart rate at baseline was 73.9 bpm NT 201, 73.9 bpm placebo and 72.7 bpm Botox®. For the final visit, these had changed to 73.0 bpm, 72.0 bpm placebo and 71.5 Botox®.
L2767: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the BEB, CD and SP study reports to determine when heart rate was measured relative to NT 201 administration. (The decrease in heart rate in the Dysport® development program was observed in a CD study in which measurements were taken 30 minutes after treatment). I reviewed the study reports for the placebo-controlled trials of BEB, CD and SP, and it was unclear when vital signs were measured during the study visit. A request has been made to the sponsor to clarify this.
L2768: In the unpooled Study (b) (4) the sponsor stated that “no clinically meaningful changes” in these parameters were observed (SP ISS 120 Day Update, pg. 57).
L2769: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the Study 0410/1 Report to compare vital sign parameters in NT 201 and placebo-treated patients. In the SP indication, the sponsor prepared a separate table for each vital sign parameter. I reviewed each of these tables (also included in the SP ISS 120 Day safety Update, Table 8.1.2.3.1.1, Table 8.1.2.3.1.2, Table 8.2.2.3.1.1, Table 8.2.2.3.1.2, Table 8.3.2.3.1.1, Table 8.3.2.3.1.2, Table 8.4.2.3.1.1, Table 8.4.2.3.1.2). The results were similar between treatment group and over time.
L2770: ## 7.4.4 Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L2771: ## 7.4.4.1 *Preclinical hERG Channel Testing*
L2772: The sponsor presented data from a hERG channel study examining the effect of NT 201 on hERG channels using patch clamp recordings. The study also contained positive controls (cisapride and terfenadine) to validate their methods. The authors reported (b) (4) (10,000 units per ml) had no effect on hERG tail currents at -20 mV. The authors concluded that negative interactions of (b) (4) and, by implication, NT-201 with hERG channels seems unlikely.
L2773: Merz noted that in the hERG channel study, a concentration of 10,000 LDU/mL (b) (4) had no effect on hERG tail currents at -20 mV. This concentration clearly exceeds the therapeutically achievable concentration in human blood even when assuming full systemic availability of the administered dose. Given a mean human blood volume of 65 mL/kg (female) to 75 mL/kg (male) and a clinically recommended maximum dose of 435 LDU per treatment the maximal concentration2 that can be achieved clinically is below 1 LDU/mL. Thus, Merz concluded that
L2774: the tested concentration in the hERG assay exceeds the maximal achievable concentration in human blood by a factor of at least 10,000. Therefore negative interactions of (b) (4) and, by implication, NT 201 with hERG channels in humans seem to be unlikely (Nonclinical Overview, pg. 11).
L2775: **Reviewer comment:** The study described above was performed using (b) (4) "from which the authors extrapolated the results to NT 201.
L2776: ## 7.4.4.2 ECGs: Other In Vivo Preclinical Studies
L2777: Merz described the following additional preclinical study related to NT 201 and cardiac function:
L2778: The effects of NT 201 and Botox® on electrocardiography (ECG) parameters were analyzed in conscious, non-restrained telemetered male Cynomolgus monkeys after a single intramuscular injection of 16 LDU/kg.
L2779: **Reviewer comment:** The FDA's Dr. Barbara Wilcox explained that that the dose of 16 LDU/kg represents 4-times the maximum human dose for CD and 9.4-times the maximum human dose for BEB.
L2780: A decrease in heart rate and interrelated variations of RR and QT intervals (most likely due to the administration under ketamine hydrochloride anesthesia) were observed 2 to 3 hours after the treatment with either NT 201 or Botox®. One week after treatment with either NT 201 or Botox®, while paralysis of the *gluteus* muscle was well established, heart rate was markedly higher than during the pre-test period or during the day of treatment. This effect was associated with concomitant effects on RR and QT interval duration. From study week 4 onwards, ECG parameters returned essentially to baseline levels. Neither NT 201 nor Botox® affected PR interval length or QRS complex duration. NT 201 induced a slight increase in the QTc interval duration corrected with Fridericia's formula, but no effect was observed on the QTc interval length corrected with Bazett's formula. As this effect was of low magnitude (less than 20 ms) and only visible after correction with Fridericia's formula, it is not considered by the sponsor to be biologically relevant. Week to week comparison of QTc values did not reveal any statistically significant difference compared to the pre-test period. Botox® induced a slight QTc interval increase from 2 to 4 hours post-treatment (Nonclinical Overview, pg. 11).
L2781: **Reviewer comment:** The FDA's pre-clinical reviewer for NT 201 noted that a 9-month study in monkeys showed a small but dose-related reduction in heart weight (absolute and relative), but that there were no in-life signals (ECG, BP, HR) or histological correlates.

L2808: ## 7.4.4.4 ECGs: BEB
L2809: Merz stated that serial electrocardiograms (ECGs) were performed in BEB Study 0003, and the results were assessed by the study Investigator and by an independent cardiologist. A brief summary of the results follows. Merz asserted that this was true for both the study drug NT 201 and the comparator drug Botox (BEB ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 55).
L2810: The sponsor stated that “the outlier analysis did not yield a large incidence rate of changes greater than 30 ms in QT or QTcF(B) for either treatment in both studies. Large changes of 60 ms were not present for the QTcF interval. The cardiologist evaluated all ECGs with respect to rhythm, conduction, and morphological abnormalities. All but one clinically relevant finding noted during the control or follow-up visits were already present at baseline.”
L2811: Merz reported that one patient in the NT 201 group developed an Intermittent Left Bundle Branch Block. On the ECG printout for this subject (age 74 years), the blood pressure was also noted (170/90 mmHg). The sponsor hypothesized that this may be an indication of arterial hypertension with underlying hypertensive heart disease for which Left Bundle Branch Blocks (intermittent or persistent) are typical. Merz reported that T wave abnormalities (mostly inverted T waves) were equally distributed amongst the treatment groups and visits. The sponsor concluded that no effect of the study drugs on the QT and QTcF(B) intervals could be detected, and the heart rate, PR interval and QRS duration were not affected by either study drug (BEB ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 55).
L2812: **Reviewer comment:** In placebo-controlled BEB Study 0433/1 study report, I was unable to find a summary table comparing ECG results in the NT 201 and placebo treatment group. Based on the text summary above, the sponsor has used primarily shift analyses and evaluation by an independent cardiologist to determine whether differences in the drug and placebo group are present. Although a summary table of values would be preferable, this is acceptable based on the preclinical and ECG results for the pooled and other indications.

L2821: ## 7.4.6 Immunogenicity
L2822: ## 7.4.6.1 Immunogenicity: Pharmacology
L2823: (b) (4)
L2824: (b) (4)
L2825: (b) (4)
L2826: **Reviewer comment:**
L2827: (b) (4);
L2828: The sections below examine the immunology from the perspective of antibody formation.
L2829: ## 7.4.6.2 Immunogenicity: Literature Review
L2830: A number of studies in the medical literature have examined the immunogenicity of botulinum toxin products. The primary of these studies are summarized here.
L2831: - An early series, looking at 559 BoNT type A treated patients between 1984 and 1992, found 24 (4.3%) with antibodies by mouse assay. Some had had only one or two injection sessions. Eight of 76 patients with follow-up (10.5%) developed resistance to drug effect. As a rule, they had had been treated with larger doses and had injections at closer intervals, especially booster injections 2 to 3 weeks after the initial session.
- Another study (by far the largest in the literature) looked at the correlation between the presence of neutralizing antibodies and non-responsiveness to treatment (Lange, et al. Clin Neuropath 32(4)213-218, 2009). Serum samples were taken from 503 patients with a variety of diagnoses treated by BoNT-A (Dysport® or BOTOX®). The selected subjects were secondary nonresponders, i.e.: initially responded but then lost the responsiveness to therapy. Fewer than half of the subjects (n = 224, 44.5%) had neutralizing antibodies. The authors concluded that non-responsiveness is not well correlated with the presence of antibodies and that non responsiveness may not reliably be used as a surrogate for the presence of antibodies. They confirmed that the presence of antibodies in these non-responders was associated with higher doses and shorter intervals between injections (for example, only 6 of 38 subjects (16%) with blepharospasm, but 175 of 362 subjects (48%) with cervical dystonia).
L2832: The authors found the presence of antibodies to be unrelated to the commercial preparation used, but found that cumulative doses higher than 6000 units were associated with a higher proportion of patients with antibodies. Of note, the mean duration of therapy until secondary nonresponsive was 41 months (Dysport®) and 45 months (BOTOX®). This also suggests that formulation differences among BoNT agents may not be relevant to the formation of antibodies or the development of non-responsiveness. An alternative plausible explanation for the loss of efficacy to BoNT products could be that actual muscle fiber damage results from the use of toxin. This has been documented by biopsy in humans (Schroeder, et al. Mov Dis 24 (10)1494-1503, 2009).
L2833: - In the NT 201 development program, antibodies were investigated (Goschel, et al Exp Neurol 147:96-102, 1997) and the Sponsor argues that antibodies were present in all non-responders, stating that non-responders who were not antibody positive subsequently responded to retreatment. However, in this paper, no study design specifications or clinical data are presented. The Sponsor demonstrated antibody development to high doses of BOTOX® but not NT 201 after biweekly injections for 12 weeks in rabbits. In a 33 week study comparing Dysport®, BOTOX® and NT 201, one animal did develop antibodies in the NT 201 group.
L2834: 4. Well-documented cases of antibodies developing after a single injection or with low dose (as in the treatment of glabellar lines) exist.
L2835: ## 7.4.6.3 Immunogenicity: Pooled Indications
L2836: During clinical development of NT 201, antibody assessments were performed using an ELISA (Enzyme-linked Immunosorbent Assay) or the improved FIA-AB (Fluorescence Immuno Assay for Antibodies) for screening. Positive samples were subsequently tested using the mouse *ex vivo* hemidiaphragm assay (HDA) for neutralizing antibodies. In the completed studies of NT 201, a total of 12 of 1080 subjects (1.1%) who were antibody negative at baseline developed neutralizing antibodies to Botulinum toxin during the course of their respective study (see Section 5.3.5.3, Table 4). Each of these 12 subjects had been treated with another Botulinum toxin prior to exposure to NT 201; no treatment-naïve subject developed antibodies to Botulinum toxin (120-Day Safety Update, pg. 76).
L2837: The sponsor's tally from all trials in the development program for all indications reveals that 0.7% of 1048 NT 201 patients and 0.8% of 354 BOTOX® patients developed anti BoNT antibodies (ISS, p. 84).
L2838: **Reviewer comment:** It is notable that in the pooled active-controlled studies (NT 201 versus Botox®) the number of patients developing antibodies in the NT 201 and Botox treatment groups were similar (0.7% NT 201, 0.8% Botox). (b) (4)
L2839: ## 7.4.6.4 Immunogenicity: Safety – BEB
L2840: Merz reported that four NT 201-treated patients in the BEB studies who were antibody-negative at baseline developed borderline values of antibodies to Botulinum toxin, including two subjects in Study 0003 and two subjects in the Study 0433/1 Main Period. The sponsor added that none of these subjects experienced TEAEs suggestive of immune-mediated events (e.g., hypersensitivity reactions, anaphylactoid reactions, or anaphylaxis). Merz further noted that all of the patients who developed antibodies had received a Botulinum toxin other than NT 201 before study entry. The sponsor stated that no subjects in the Study 0433/2 OLEX Period developed antibodies to Botulinum toxin (BEB ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 61).
L2841: Although the four patients did not experience AEs suggestive of a hypersensitivity reaction, the four subjects who developed anti-Botulinum antibodies did experience other AEs: two experienced TEAEs. Subject 2133 in Study 0433/1 experienced mild malaise which resolved. Subject 2153 in Study 0433/1 experienced the events worsening of hypertension, hemorrhoid pain, nasopharyngitis, and left ear infection; all of which resolved. (BEB ISS, 120 Day Update, pg. 61).
L2842: In Trial 0433/1 (placebo controlled) and Trial 0003 (active control with BOTOX®), the sponsor screened antibodies by ELISA and confirmed with the mouse hemidiaphragm bioassay. Antibody
L2843: titers &gt; 3.58 mU/mL and hemidiaphragm paralysis times &gt; 64 minutes were considered positive. In these two trials, 2 of 222 patients (1%) had antibodies at baseline (2 others were “borderline”). In Trial 0433/1 two subjects developed transient “borderline” antibodies which were not present at the final visit. In Trial 0003, one NT 201 subject became positive and another “borderline” at the final visit. In Trial 0003, two subjects developed antibodies in the BOTOX® arm; they had had 21 and 31 previous injection sessions, respectively. There were no clinical phenomena which could be attributed to this development.
L2844: ## 7.4.6.5 Immunogenicity: Safety – CD
L2845: Four subjects who were antibody-negative at screening developed antibodies to Botulinum toxin during participation in the Study 0408/1 Main Period. Three of these subjects did not experience any TEAEs. The fourth subject, Subject 1257, experienced bilateral leg pain, bilateral leg weakness, bilateral arm pain and bilateral arm weakness approximately one week after treatment with NT 201.
L2846: During the Study 0408/2 Extension Period, four additional subjects developed antibodies to Botulinum toxin. Three of these subjects reported TEAEs (CD ISS, pg. 56).
L2847: - **Subject 1053** had a positive FIA result at all assessments, a negative HDA result at baseline, a positive result before injection 3 on 25-Jun-2007, and a negative HDA result at the last study visit (August 2007). This subject experienced two TEAEs, mild torticollis and moderate neck pain, both of which occurred approximately two weeks after the antibody-positive sample was obtained (July 2007).
- **Subject 1079** had a positive FIA result and a negative HDA result at baseline, and a positive (borderline) result at the 6th injection visit. This subject experienced the following TEAEs during the Extension Period (where the subject had multiple episodes of an event, the worst intensity and relationship are given): head titubation, haemoglobin decreased, gastric ulcer, neck pain (3 episode), headache, optic neuropathy and dysphagia (two episodes).
- **Subject 1313** had a negative FIA and HDA result at baseline, and a positive (borderline) HDA result at the 4th injection visit. The subject experienced the TEAEs respiratory tract infection (moderate, not related) and pain in extremity (moderate, not related) during the Extension Period.
L2848: ## 7.4.6.6 Immunogenicity: Safety – SP
L2849: In the Study 0410/1 Main Period and 0410/2 OLEX Period, antibody determination was performed at each visit before injection of NT 201, 4 weeks after the second injection with NT 201 during the Study 0410/2 (OLEX Period), and at the end of the trial. In these studies, Merz reported that 47 subjects received the first injection, 136 subjects received the second injection, 121 subjects received the third, 100 subjects received the fourth, 51 subjects received the fifth, and 7 subjects received the sixth injection.
L2850: Merz reported that no patient in these studies developed neutralizing antibodies to Botulinum toxin, despite receiving maximum doses of 500 U NT 201 (the median dose at each injection was 360 to 400 U), and up to 6 injections. Merz asserted that a high proportion of patients (73.6%) in this study had never been treated with a Botulinum toxin preparation prior to the study (120-Day ISS, pg. 57).
L2851: ## 7.4.6.7 Immunogenicity: Reviewer Conclusions
L2852: **Reviewer comment:** Only four of the 12 subjects who developed antibodies to Botulinum toxin experienced TEAEs. None of the TEAEs associated with anti-Botulinum antibodies were suggestive of immune-mediated events (e.g., hypersensitivity reactions, anaphylactoid reactions, or anaphylaxis). The sponsor asserted that most of the subjects who developed neutralizing antibodies showed a therapeutic response to treatment, and for many, the positive antibody titers were a transient finding or false positive because borderline results are measured. Although subjects in Study 0410 (SP) received up to six doses of NT 201 in a high dose of a maximum of 500 U, none of these subjects developed neutralizing antibodies. Finally, in studies comparing NT 201 and Botox®, the number of patients developing antibodies in the NT 201 and Botox treatment groups were essentially the same (0.7% NT 201, 0.8% Botox). NT 201 therefore does not demonstrate a strong signal for clinically significant immune responses, although cases of anaphylaxis have been reported during the postmarketing use of NT 201 outside of the United States.
L2853: (b) (4)
L2854: ## 7.5 Other Safety Explorations: AE Risk by Study Location
L2855: **Reviewer comment:** A pattern has been observed in various drug development programs in which studies conducted in North America report higher rates of adverse events than similar studies conducted elsewhere. In some development program, this difference affected only common adverse events, but in others it has affected both serious and common adverse events. In some cases, the difference has been significant enough that the FDA has requested separate adverse event tables in labeling for North American and non-North American studies.
L2856: I therefore examined the risk of serious and common adverse events in several North American and non-North American studies within the NT 201 development program. The results are shown in the table below. All of the SP studies were conducted outside North America, so SP could not be included in the analysis. In other development programs which have demonstrated higher AE rates in the North American studies, the effect is often more pronounced for AEs thought to be related to the drug, and less so for AEs not believed to be drug-related. In each indication I have selected AEs from each of these categories.

L2859: **Reviewer comment:** As observed in other drug development programs, the analysis above does show a higher number of reported adverse events in the North American studies compared to those outside of North America. In many instances the risk of the common adverse events (overall and for specific indications) was approximately two- to three-fold higher in the North American countries compared to the non-North American countries. Consistent with the pattern seen in other drug development programs, the effect is more pronounced for the common adverse
L2860: *events than for serious adverse events. This difference in common adverse events should be considered in the selection of tables to be used in the US labeling.*
L2861: ## 7.5.1 Dose-Dependency for Adverse Events
L2862: **Reviewer comment:** *Because NT 201 is administered as an intermittent single injection, the examination of drug exposure in relation to adverse events should encompass both the dose of the injection and the total number of injections. The following sections therefore discuss both of these dose-related issues for the total NT 201 data and by indication (BEB, CD, and SP).*
L2863: ## 7.5.1.1 Dose-Dependency for AEs: Pooled Indications
L2864: **Reviewer comment:** *The sponsor presented a table20 showing common adverse events by dose group for all the pooled studies within the NT 201 development program. I do not include this table here as the adverse events are specific to the indications (for example, ptosis for BEB or dysphagia for CD) and pooling the groups dilutes the true risk in the relevant indication and providing misleading information within a dose-response analysis. In addition, the doses of NT 201 varied significantly by indications. For these reasons the dose-dependence of AEs should be examined by indication, as opposed to combined.*
L2865: ## 7.5.1.2 Dose-Dependency for AEs: BEB
L2866: **Dose:** Merz stated that in the Study 0433/1 Main Period and 0433/2 OLEX Period, the incidence of SAEs was higher in subjects who received 10 to 40 U of NT 201, compared with those who received &gt;40 U of NT 201. Six of 27 subjects in the 10 to 40 U group (22.2%), compared with 4 of 79 subjects in the &gt;40 U group (5.1%), experienced SAEs. The sponsor noted that although it appears that the incidence of SAEs does not increase with higher NT 201 doses in the treatment of BEB, because of the small number of subjects with SAEs, it is not possible to draw reliable conclusions based on these data (BEB ISS – 120 Day Update, pg. 23).

L2910: **Reviewer comment:** In the SOCs in which there were the most number of patients receiving each injection (Musculoskeletal and Connective Tissue Disorders, Gastrointestinal Disorders, and General Disorders), the risk of adverse event decreases as the number of injection increases. As noted elsewhere in this review, one explanation for this trend is that patients who experienced bothersome adverse events withdrew from the study before they received additional injections, leaving the patients most resistant to adverse events to receive the most injections (survivor bias). This theory is supported by the decreasing number of patients with each subsequent injection.

L2920: **Reviewer comment:** As per the table above, although the total patients with adverse events increased across dose groups, for individual common AEs no strong dose response patterns were observed, which may be due to the instability in the analysis stemming from the small number of events in each dose strata.
L2921: ## 7.5.2 Time Dependency for Adverse Events
L2922: ## 7.5.2.1 Time Dependency for Adverse Events: Pooled Indications
L2923: Comparisons of the three Phase 3, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled studies (Study 0408/1 [CD], Study 0433/1 [BEB], and Study 0410/1 [SP]) indicate that NT 201 has a time to onset (mean of 6.0 – 7.7 days), time to waning (mean of 6.5 – 10.6 weeks), and duration of effect (mean of 73.9 – 97.7 days)(Clinical Overview, pg. 17).
L2924: **Reviewer comment:** Due to the small number of adverse events and the relatively wide range of therapeutic action of NT 201, although the pooled placebo-controlled studies did not find a difference in the NT 201 and placebo-treated patients with regard to the most frequent time category for reporting events (2 to 4 weeks) a complete time-dependency analysis was not possible.
L2925: ## 7.5.3 Drug-Demographic Interactions
L2926: **Reviewer comment:** For all the indications, including the pooled indications, there were too few non-Caucasian subjects to sub-analyze the adverse event data by race. Race is therefore not included in the discussion of the individual and pooled indications below.
L2927: ## 7.5.3.1 Drug-Demographic Interactions: Pooled Indications
L2928: For gender analysis, in the placebo-controlled studies of the three neurologic indications, Merz reported that “male subjects who received NT 201 had a slightly higher incidence of SAEs, compared with the male subjects who received placebo.” However, the sponsor noted that because the number of subjects in all groups who experienced SAEs was small, it is difficult to draw reliable conclusions based on these data (Pooled Neurologic Indications, 120 Day ISS, pg. 44).
L2929: For age and common AEs, Merz report that when the incidence rates of TEAEs in the three age groups are compared, subjects &gt;65 years of age treated with NT 201 reported more events of dysphagia, dry mouth and eyelid ptosis, compared with subjects in the other age groups treated with NT 201. However, the number of subjects in each of the groups is small, making it difficult to draw reliable conclusions based on these data.
L2930: ## 7.5.3.2 Drug-Demographics Interactions: BEB
L2931: For SAEs, there were too few events in the BEB placebo-controlled trials to construct an analysis based on gender. In the pooled BEB studies (the Study 0433/1 Main Period and Study 0003), 3 of 164 female subjects treated with NT 201 (1.8%), and 1 of 22 female subjects treated with placebo (4.6%), experienced an SAE. No male subjects treated with NT 201 in the pooled studies experienced serious events (BEB ISS – 120 Day Update, pg. 23).
L2932: For SAEs in the Study 0433/1 Main Period and 0433/2 OLEX Period, Merz reported that the incidence of SAEs in male and female subjects receiving NT 201 was similar. Four of 37 male subjects (10.8%) and 6 of 69 female subjects (8.7%) experienced SAEs (BEB ISS, 120 Day Update).
L2933: For SAEs, there were two few events to meaningfully examine the effect of age with NT 201 treatment. In the Study 0433/1 Main Period, the only subject who experienced an SAE was 75 years of age. In the Study 0433/1 Main Period and 0433/2 OLEX Period, in subjects who received NT 201, the incidence of SAEs was similar across age groups. One of 12 subjects ≤50 years of age (8.3%), 5 of 52 subjects &gt;50 to 65 years of age (9.6%), and 4 of 42 subjects &gt;65 years of age (9.5%) experienced SAEs (BEB 120 Day Update, pg. 23).
L2934: **Reviewer comment:** The small number of AEs in the BEB indication precluded analysis by race.
L2935: ## 7.5.3.3 *Drug-Demographics Interactions: CD*
L2936: For SAEs, in the Study 0408/1 Main Period and Study 0408/2 Extension Period (analyzed together), Merz found that 15 of 227 subjects (6.6%) had experienced SAEs, including 8 of 79 male subjects (10.1%), and 7 of 148 female subjects (4.7%)(CD ISS, pg. 32).
L2937: For age, in the Study 0408/1 Main Period, the sponsor reported that 2 of 94 subjects (2.1%) in the NT 201 group who were \le 50 years of age, 2 of 110 subjects (1.8%) in the NT 201 group who were &gt;50 65 years of age, no subject in NT 201 group who was &gt;65 years of age, and no subject in the placebo group experienced an SAE.
L2938: **Reviewer comment:** *For age, gender and race, the number of patients and SAEs was too small to reliably detect any interaction.*
L2939: For common adverse events, in the Study 0408/1 Main Period, Merz stated that in male subjects, NT 201-treated subjects, compared with placebo-treated subjects, had a higher incidence of common or very common TEAEs (difference \ge 3\%) in every SOC, and most PTs (difference \ge 2\%). For female subjects, the PTs in which NT 201-treated subjects, compared with placebo-treated subjects, had a higher incidence (difference \ge 2\%) of common or very common events were, nausea, muscular weakness, neck pain, and musculoskeletal pain. Female patient treated with NT 201 appear to have greater incidence rates for the event dysphagia, compared with male subjects (CD ISS, pg 43).
L2940: **Reviewer comment:** *The sponsor prepared a table of common adverse event stratified by gender (Table 19, CD ISS), which I reviewed. Although there are a number of adverse events that are somewhat more common in one gender over the other, there are too few patients in each subgroup to support an analysis on the level of Preferred Term events. In addition, there is no biologic rationale to suspect that one gender would be affected differentially than another.*
L2941: In the pooled CD studies (the Study 0408/1 Main Period, and Studies 0013 and 9801), the sponsor reported that for subjects in the NT 201 group TEAE incidence was highest in the middle aged group (\le 50 years of age: 64/212 subjects, 30.2%; &gt;50-65 years of age: 83/171 subjects, 48.5%; &gt;65 years of age: 19/48 subjects, 39.6%). At the SOC level, subjects in the &gt;50-65 years of age group had a higher incidence of events in the Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders, Infections and infestations, and Respiratory, thoracic and mediastinal disorders SOCs, compared with the other age groups. At the PT level, Merz stated that the incidence of events in the NT 201 treatment group was similar across age groups, but the number of events was too small to allow for reliable assessment of the possible relationship between age and the incidence of adverse events in subjects receiving NT 201 (CD ISS, pg. 48).
L2942: **Reviewer comment:** *As with the analysis by gender, there were too few patients in each subgroup to support a reliable analysis. The number of patients in the over 65 age group was particularly small.*
L2943: Merz stated that there were too few non-Caucasian patients to perform any analyses by race (CD ISS, pg 22).
L2944: ## *7.5.3.4 Drug-Demographic Interactions: SP*
L2945: Overall, within the NT 201 and the placebo groups, more female subjects experienced TEAEs. For both male and female subjects in the Study 0410/1 Main Period, the incidence of TEAEs was similar in the placebo and NT 201 groups. At the PT level, the number of TEAEs was too small to permit a reliable assessment of the relationship between gender and the incidences of TEAEs with NT 201 administration.
L2946: When Studies 0410/1 Main Period and 0410/2 OLEX Period are analyzed together, female patients had a slightly higher overall incidence of TEAEs, compared with male subjects. Thirty-four of 52 female subjects (65.4%) and 56 of 95 male subjects (59.0%) experienced at least one TEAE. At the SOC level, a higher percent of female than male subjects (difference \ge 5\%) experienced events in the Investigations (10 subjects, 19.2% vs. 4 subjects, 4.2%), Infections and infestations 13 subjects, 25% vs. 16 subjects, 16.8%).
L2947: Merz stated that in the Study 0410 Main Period, TEAE risk did not increase with increasing age. No NT 201-treated subject over 65 years of age experienced a severe or a serious adverse event.
L2948: *Reviewer comment: I reviewed the source tables referenced by the sponsor for the statements above (SP ISS pgs. 38-40) and found the statements to be accurate.*
L2949: ## 7.5.3.3.4 Drug-Demographics Interactions: Reviewer Conclusions
L2950: *Reviewer comment: In the various BEB, CD and SP studies within the NT 201 development program, analysis by age and gender showed minor variations in AE risk. However, due to the minimal change and the small number of patients in the subgroups, no demographic factor was demonstrated to be an effect modifier.*
L2951: ## 7.5.4 Drug-Disease Interactions

The sponsor noted, however, that at the SOC and PT levels, the number of individual SAEs was low (most events occurred in only one subject), and Merz asserted that no pattern of increased incidence of certain events or types of events in subjects with severe disease at baseline is apparent (as shown in the table below).

L2959: Merz explained that because the dose of NT 201 varied widely across indications, with the oldest (i.e., the BEB population) and sickest (i.e., the post-stroke SP population) patient represented primarily in the lowest and highest dose groups, it is difficult to assess the incidence of SAEs by total dose group in the pooled, controlled studies. However, when SAEs were examined by total NT 201 dose at the SOC and PT levels, the number of individual SAEs was low (most events occurred in only one patient), and the sponsor believes that no pattern of increased incidence of certain events is apparent (ISS, pg. 42).
L2960: **Reviewer comment:** I agree with the sponsor that examining SAEs in the pooled, NT 201 studies leads to indeterminate results. Common adverse events are presented by indication below.
L2961: ## 7.5.4.2 Drug-Disease Interaction: BEB
L2962: In the Study 0433/1 Main Period, in the NT 201 treatment group, Merz reported that patients with mild disease at baseline had higher incidence rates of eyelid ptosis, compared with patients with moderate or severe disease at baseline. There was no clear pattern of increased incidences for TEAEs in any group of NT 201-treated patients; however, the number of subjects with
L2963: TEAEs in each group is small, making it difficult to draw reliable conclusions based on these data (BEB ISS, pg. 37).
L2964: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed Table 19 within the BEB ISS which examined AEs by disease severity, and found no clinically meaningful pattern.
L2965: ## 7.5.4.3 Drug-Disease Interaction: CD
L2966: For SAEs, in the Study 0408/1 placebo-controlled Main Period, in the NT 201 treatment group, Merz reported that no patient with mild disease at baseline, 2 of 81 subjects (2.5%) with moderate disease, and 2 of 39 subjects (5.1%) with severe disease experienced SAEs. The sponsor stated that no subject in the placebo group experienced a serious event. The sponsor added that a similar finding is noted in the Botox group, with 1 of 67 subjects (1.5%) with mild disease, 2 of 137 subjects (1.5%) with moderate disease, and 3 of 40 subjects (7.5%) with severe disease, experiencing SAEs. Merz asserted that when SAEs are examined by SOC and PT, however, no pattern of increased incidence of any particular event, with increased baseline disease severity, is noted (CD ISS, pg. 33).
L2967: For common adverse events, Merz stated that patients in the NT 201 group who had mild disease at baseline had a higher incidence of certain TEAEs (e.g., muscular weakness, musculoskeletal pain, headache, injection site pain, sinusitis, asthma) compared with the respective placebo group, than subjects in the NT 201 group with moderate or severe baseline disease. The sponsor hypothesized that the reason for the decreased incidence rates with increasing baseline severity may be due to the fact that moderate and severe CD often have substantial muscle hypertrophy due to the continuous muscle activity. Merz noted that botulinum neurotoxin administration usually targets these hypertrophic muscles (CD ISS, pg. 54).
L2968: **Reviewer comment:** One would expect the preceding explanation of the inverse relationship between common AEs and disease severity to have also applied to SAEs, which was not the case. Another explanation is that, as with other subgroup analyses, the small number of patients creates unstable results that can be heavily influenced by events, or lack thereof, only a few patients.
L2969: ## 7.5.4.4 Drug-Disease Interaction: SP
L2970: Merz stated that the majority (78%) of subjects treated with NT 201 in the Main Period of the study had experienced an ischemic stroke, with a mean time since diagnosis of SP of approximately 5 years. In the Study 0410 Main Period, TEAE risk did not increase with baseline disease severity (0410/1 Final Safety Report, pg. 67).
L2971: **Reviewer comment:** I reviewed the tables referenced by the sponsor and did not a clinically meaningful trend of adverse events based on baseline disease severity measurements.
L2972: ## 7.5.5 Drug-Drug Interactions
L2973: Merz stated that the effect of Botulinum toxin could “hypothetically” be potentiated by aminoglycoside antibiotics or other medicinal products that interfere with neuromuscular transmission, e.g., tubocurarine type muscle relaxants. The sponsor referenced three studies in support of this statement Santos 1981, Wang et al. 198421 and Yamada et al. 198622. Therefore, Merz cautioned that the concomitant use of NT 201 with aminoglycosides or spectinomycin requires special care, and peripheral muscle relaxants should be used with caution.
L2974: **Reviewer comment:** It was not possible to assess the effect of concomitant use of aminoglycosides, spectinomycin or muscle relaxants with respect to adverse event occurrence in the NT 201 development program. I reviewed the three studies referenced by the sponsor and concur that due to the theoretical risk a statement noting the potential interaction should be included in labeling.
L2975: ## 7.6 Additional Safety Evaluations: Pre-Treatment with other Botulinum Toxin Products
L2976: Merz defined patients who had not been treated with Botulinum toxin for the study indication for at least one year before study entry as treatment-naïve, and patient who had been treated with Botulinum toxin for the study indication within one year as pre-treated, as agreed at the pre-BLA meeting with FDA (FDA meeting minutes, July, 2007). These definitions were somewhat different for studies examining botulinum antibodies, with “pre-treated” defined as treatment with a Botulinum toxin of any serotype, for any indication, at any time before study entry, and “treatment-naïve” defined as no treatment with a Botulinum toxin for any indication at any time before study entry (CD ISS, pg. 15).
L2977: ## 7.6.1 Botulinum Toxin Pre-Treatment
L2978: ## 7.6.1.1 Botulinum Toxin Pre-Treatment: BEB
L2979: Merz stated that there were very few patients who were Botulinum toxin naïve in the BEB studies, thus no assessment of the relationship between treatment status and the incidence of AEs could be performed (BEB ISS, 120-Day Update, pg. 23).
L2980: ## 7.6.1.2 Botulinum Toxin Pre-Treatment: CD
L2981: The percent of patients with prior treatment with other botulinum toxin products varied by study protocol. In the Study 0408/1 (CD) Main Period, Merz stated that 87 of 159 subjects (54.7%)

Int J Clin Pharmacol Ther Toxicol 1986; 24(3): 130-138.
L2983: were pre-treated and 72 of 159 subjects (45.3%) were treatment-naïve. The percent of pre-treated and treatment-naïve subjects in the placebo group was similar to the NT 201 group. As per protocol, in Study 9801 Merz reported that 100% of subjects were treatment-naïve (not treated with any Botulinum toxin one year prior to trial entry) and in Study 0013, 100% of subjects were pre-treated with Botulinum toxin (CD ISS, pg. 15).
L2984: Among the CD studies, Merz stated that only Study 0408 enrolled both Botulinum toxin pre-treated and treatment-naïve subjects. In the Main Period of this study, the four subjects who experienced SAEs were pre-treated. In the study 0408/2 Extension Period, 11 additional patients experienced common AEs. These include 8 of 11 subjects (72.7%) who were pre-treated and 3 of 11 subjects (27.3%) who were treatment-naïve at the beginning of the study 0408/1 Main Period (CD ISS, pg. 32).
L2985: **Reviewer comment:** Although pre-treated patients in the CD indication seem to be over-represented among patients who experienced AEs (100% of patients in the SAEs, 73% of patients with common AEs and 55% within the general study population), the number of patients is small and the comparison is not controlled for dose or disease severity.

L2997: **Reviewer conclusions:** In the NT 201 development program, in the CD indication AEs were more frequent in patients with previous treatment with botulinum toxin, whereas the reverse was true in the SP indication (There were two few events in the BEB indication to evaluate events by treatment status). Due to the small number of events and probably confounding by disease severity and dose, no evidence of a difference in AE risk by treatment status is apparent.
L2998: ## 7.6.2 Human Carcinogenicity
L2999: The proposed NT 201 labeling states that “No genotoxicity, carcinogenicity or pre- and postnatal development studies have been conducted with Xeomin.”
L3000: In the “Nonclinical Overview” (pg. 35), the sponsor elaborated on their rationale for not performing carcinogenicity studies.
L3001: No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted with NT(b)(4)201, in accordance with the guidance in (ICH S6 1997): “Standard carcinogenicity bioassays are generally
L3002: inappropriate for biotechnology-derived pharmaceuticals. However, product-specific assessment of carcinogenic potential may still be needed depending on duration of clinical dosing, patient population and/or biological activity of the product (e.g. growth factors, immunosuppressive agents, etc.). When there is a concern about carcinogenic potential a variety of approaches may be considered to evaluate risk."
L3003: Merz asserted "there is no scientific cause for concern regarding a carcinogenic potential of BoNT/A and NT(b) (4)201."
L3004: **Reviewer comment:** Dr. Lois Freed within the DNP was consulted regarding the sponsor's assertion that carcinogenicity studies were not required. Dr. Freed confirmed that such studies are difficult to perform for botulinum toxin products and the sponsor was indeed exempt from performing them.
L3005: According to the integrated summary of safety, in the pooled, placebo-controlled trials, treatment-emergent adverse events in the "Neoplasms benign, malignant and unspecified" SOC occurred in 5 of 701 subjects (0.7%) in the NT 201 group, compared with 4 of 346 subjects (1.2%) in the placebo group. The events that occurred in the NT 201 group were melanocytic naevus, dysplastic naevus syndrome, haemangioma, lentigo maligna stage unspecified, and skin cancer (1 subject, 0.1%, each). The events in the placebo group were melanocytic naevus (2 subjects, 0.6%), basal cell carcinoma, and skin papilloma (1 subject, 0.3%, each).
L3006: **Reviewer Comment:** In the pooled, placebo-controlled trials, the risk of neoplasm was higher in the placebo group (1.2%) compared to NT 201 group (0.7%). The cancers occurring in the placebo-controlled trials were largely skin cancers or precancerous lesions, which are also the most common form of cancer observed within the general population.

L3008: ## 7.6.3 Human Reproduction and Pregnancy Data
L3009: Merz performed several preclinical studies examining the effect of prenatal NT 201 exposure. These were performed in rabbits and rats and did not reveal any adverse reproductive toxicity finding up to dose levels exhibiting maternal toxicity. Maternal NOAEL was 30 LDU/kg and embryotoxicity NOAEL was 90 LDU/kg.
L3010: (b) (4)
L3011: This was also their rationale for not performing any peri- and post-natal toxicology studies for NT 201.
L3012: Merz noted that there was limited clinical experience of NT 201 use during pregnancy. The sponsor stated that one patient in the ongoing Extension Period of Study 0408/2 (CD), one patient in Study 0013 (CD), and one patient in Study 0520/2 (GFL) became pregnant following exposure to NT 201. The subject in Study 0013 had an elective abortion “for personal reasons.” Brief narratives for the other two pregnancies follow (ISS, pg. 71).
L3013: - **Subject 1232:** This 35-year-old female with CD was enrolled in the Study 0408/2 Extension Period. She received a single dose of NT 201 120 U in the Main Period of the study, and
L3014: received three injections of NT 201 during the Extension Period. The last injection was on November 16, 2007. The patient stopped taking birth control pills between the last week of November and the first week of December, 2007. She did not inform the study Investigator of this change. She had her last menstrual period on [REDACTED] (b) (6) On February 26, 2008, the patient's participation in the study was discontinued because of pregnancy. On [REDACTED] (b) (6), the subject gave birth at [REDACTED] (b) (6) gestation to a healthy boy weighing 8 lbs (3.63 kg). Mother and child "were doing well."
L3015: 2. **Subject 2117:** This 43-year-old female with GFL was enrolled in the Study 0520/2 Extension Period. She received the first dose of NT 201 20 U in the Main Period of the study on November 20, 2006, and the most recent dose of NT 201 20 U on July 9, 2007. At Visit 10 of the Main Period (August 30, 2007), the patient's pregnancy test was positive. The subject was not treated further in the study. The pregnancy was followed to outcome. A healthy baby was born on [REDACTED] (b) (6); and "the mother and child were doing well."
L3016: The sponsor stated that in the pooled, placebo-controlled studies, no TEAEs in the Pregnancy, puerperium and perinatal conditions SOC occurred in any treatment group. [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L3017: **Reviewer comment:** In the Dysport® development program, there were more cases of pregnancy during treatment. The following is exempted from Dr. Stone's23 safety review of Dysport®:
L3018: "There were 37 cases detailing possible exposure during pregnancy, 11 in clinical trials and 26 spontaneous. No cases of exposure during pregnancy or lactation were reported from Category 1, 2 or 3 studies. Eleven cases were received from Category 4 studies. Of these, 7 subjects were known to have received Dysport and four cases occurred in ongoing studies which had not been unblinded at data cutoff. These eleven pregnancies are summarized in Table 7.1.1.14-1. Women with unfavorable outcomes were older and had slightly higher average total exposure to Dysport."
L3019: Clearly, although no bad outcomes were reported in the NT 201 development program, the number of pregnancies was too small to make conclusions about the effect of prenatal NT 201 exposure. Given that the indications for NT 201 use are not life-threatening, known to affect pregnancies in themselves or progressive, from a risk-benefit standpoint I would recommend that patients known to be pregnant be advised not to receive NT 201. For reference, Dysport® is labeled as Pregnancy Category C.
L3020: **Reviewer comment:** The meeting minutes for the Type B Pre-BLA of December 12, 2008 stated that "Discussion of the administrative, quality, preclinical and clinical questions related to the BLA for the proposed indications cervical dystonia [REDACTED] (b) (4) FDA emphasized the need to conduct pre and post natal development studies and stated that the Sponsor
L3021: *should provide written justification if they are unable to provide these studies.” The FDA’s Dr. Barbara Wilcox reported that the sponsor had not completed a pre- and post-natal development study as requested at the meeting, and that their justification was not adequate. She noted that a PMR to address the deficiency was planned.*
L3022: ## 7.6.4 Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L3023: Merz noted that the indications for which NT 201 was studied, and particularly BEB, CD and SP, occur almost exclusively in older patients. For example, post-stroke spasticity and cervical dystonia (which has a typical onset age between 40 and 50 years) are rare in the pediatric population. Blepharospasm essentially does not occur in pediatric patients. Merz stated they are requesting a waiver of the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) requirement for pediatric studies in CD (ISS, pg. 87). However, the meeting minutes from December 12, 2008 has the following comment from FDA: “Post-marketing safety data from approved botulinum toxins suggest that botulinum toxin effects may, in some cases, be observed beyond the site of local injection. The risk for this is probably greatest in children treated for spasticity but can also occur in adults treated for spasticity. Therefore, we will be requesting a placebo controlled clinical trial in children with lower-limb spasticity, and a placebo controlled clinical trial in adults with lower limb spasticity as post-marketing requirements.”
L3024: ## 7.6.5 Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal and Rebound
L3025: ## 7.6.5.1 Overdose
L3026: The sponsor explained that the signs and symptoms of overdose of NT 201 are not evident immediately after injection. A consensus statement published in 2001 by the Working Group on Civilian Biodefense [Arnon et al. 2001] notes that persons potentially exposed to Botulinum toxin should be closely supervised for up to several weeks, and those with symptoms of overdose should be treated promptly with anti-toxin and supportive care which may include mechanical ventilation for weeks or months (ISS, pg. 99).
L3027: Merz summarized the symptoms of botulinum toxin overdose as severe neuromuscular paralysis manifesting as may generalized weakness, ptosis, diplopia, swallowing and speech difficulties, and/or paralysis of the respiratory muscles, resulting in aspiration pneumonia (Clinical Overview, pg. 33).
L3028: By the time overdose symptoms occur, Merz stated that treatment with anti-toxin is no longer be effective because the neurotoxin has already irreversibly blocked the transmitter release. Compounds releasing acetylcholine (e.g., physostigmine, guanidine, 3,4-diaminopyridine) might be effective. The sponsor noted, however, that there is no specific antidote to Botulinum toxin.
L3029: Merz stated that the lethal amount of crystalline BoNT/A for a 70 kilogram human is estimated at 0.09 \mu\text{g} intravenous (IV) or IM, or 70 \mu\text{g} applied orally. For comparison, a vial with 100 U of NT 201 contains 0.6 ng of BoNT/A, or less than 1/100 of the estimated human lethal dose following IV or IM administration [Arnon et al. 2001](ISS, pg. 98).
L3030: **Reviewer comment:** Although there were no cases of systemic spread within the Xeomin development program, there have been cases of systemic spread with other botulinum toxins when administered at high doses. The Dysport® label carries a “Black Box” warning noting that Dysport® “and all botulinum toxins products may spread from the area of injection to produce symptoms consistent with botulinum toxin effects.” Although no cases of systemic spread were observed in the NT 201 development program, I recommend that this boxed warning be included in the NT 201 labeling as well.
L3031: ## 7.6.5.2 Abuse
L3032: The sponsor stated that there were no reports of drug abuse of Botulinum toxin type A preparations. They further added that based on the mode of action, there is no reason to assume a potential for abuse or dependence (ISS, pg. 98).
L3033: **Reviewer comment:** I agree that based both on the post-marketing results of other botulinum toxins and the apparent lack of overlap between botulinum toxin's mechanism of action and known abuse mechanisms, I agree that there is little evidence of an abuse potential for NT 201.
L3034: ## 7.6.5.3 Withdrawal and Rebound
L3035: Merz stated that there were no cases of withdrawal symptoms reported within the NT 201 development program, and that the nature of action of botulinum toxin was inconsistent with drugs which cause a withdrawal syndrome.
L3036: **Reviewer comment:** Although its effects last for weeks, NT 201 is administered as a single intermittent injection, so withdrawal seems unlikely. In addition, although the patient's pretreatment symptoms will return as the effect of NT 201 decreases with time, there is no evidence to expect the symptoms will increase in a rebound fashion, and I found no evidence of this during the safety review.
L3037: ## 7.7 Additional Submissions / Safety Issues
L3038: ## 7.7.1 NT 201- Botox® Comparison Studies
L3039: Merz performed Studies 0013 and 9801 within the CD indication to compare the safety profile of NT 201 in comparison with Botox®. In both of these studies, the sponsor concluded that NT 201 and Botox were shown to have a similar safety profile (CD ISS, pg. 58).
L3040: In Study 0013, Merz stated that 231 subjects were treated with NT 201 and 232 subjects with Botox®. SAEs were observed in 4 of 231 (1.7%) subjects in the NT 201 group, and in 5 of 232 (2.2%) subjects in the Botox group. The most common TEAE in both treatment groups was dysphagia. Only one patient (in the Botox® group) discontinued due to an adverse event (pathological fracture of ankle)(CD ISS, pg. 58).
L3041: In Study 9801, the sponsor stated that 41 subjects were randomized to receive one of three doses of NT 201, and 12 subjects received Botox®. The sponsor reported that 2 of 41 subjects receiving NT 201, and 1 of 12 subjects receiving Botox®, experienced SAEs. The patient in the Botox group died (of metastatic colon cancer). Merz stated that the incidence of TEAEs was higher in the Botox® group than in the NT 201 groups. Dysphagia was reported by three subjects in the NT 201 treatment groups (7.3%), and no subject in the Botox® group (0%). Merz asserted that no clinically significant changes in laboratory values were observed at Day 14 and Day 120 in the NT 201 dose groups or the Botox group (CD ISS, pg. 58).
L3042: ## 8 Postmarket Experience
L3043: At the time of the BLA filing, NT 201 was approved in Argentina, Canada, Mexico, Uruguay, and several European countries for the indications BEB and CD in adults. It was first approved in Germany in 2005. In Canada, NT 201 is also approved for the indication post-stroke SP of the upper limb. In Argentina, NT 201 is additionally approved for a variety of other indications including focal spasticities, strabismus, and the temporary improvement of glabellar frown lines (ISS, pg. 99).
L3044: The sponsor submitted a Periodic Safety Update Report (PSUR#6) encompassing the time period until July 1, 2009. During the year covered by this PSUR, (b) (4) vials of Xeomin® were sold. Assuming 1 vial per patient and injections every 3 to 4 months, the Sponsor estimates (b) (4) patients exposed. An additional 1031 subjects in clinical trials were also exposed to NT 201. Cumulative exposure in clinical trials and since the first marketed use of Xeomin® on May 31, 2005 is estimated at (b) (4) vials and about (b) (4) patients. No marketing authorizations have been withdrawn or suspended.24
L3045: Merz reported that they searched the NT 201 pharmacovigilance database for all safety data from marketed use of NT 201 and other Botulinum neurotoxin products through January 29, 2009. The sponsor's pharmacovigilance data consisted of spontaneous reports or reports from the literature (Merz noted that no consumer reports had been received as of January 2009). (ISS, pg. 99-100).
L3046: Merz found that the following adverse events were reported to the database during post-approval use of NT 201: eye swelling, eyelid edema, dysphagia, nausea, injection site pain, injection site reaction, hypersensitivity including allergic dermatitis, skin rash, erythema, drug eruptions,
L3047: 24 This paragraph adapted from the NT 201 BEB Efficacy Review prepared by Dr. Ken Bergman, pg. 109.
L3048: herpes zoster, muscular weakness, muscle spasm, dysarthria, and myalgia. The sponsor specified that events were selected for inclusion in this document based on either frequency (i.e., events occurring in \ge 2 subjects) or medical importance (ISS, pg. 100).
L3049: **Reviewer comment:** *The majority of the events above, such as injection site reaction and pain, dysphagia, and muscular weakness/spasm, are those expected with use of a botulinum toxin product.*
L3050: In addition, Merz found the following adverse reactions have been identified for subjects receiving other Botulinum toxin type A complex preparations (i.e., not NT 201) during post-approval period of NT 201: "madarosis," blurred vision, dry eye, optic neuropathy, angle closure glaucoma, mydriasis, ptosis, ophthalmoplegia, oculomotor weakness, palatal palsy, dyspepsia, dysgeusia, dry mouth, hoarseness, necrotizing fasciitis, fibrous histiocytoma, muscular weakness, asthenia, facial paresis, stridor, dizziness, headache, and overdose (ISS, pg 101).
L3051: ## 9 APPENDICES
L3052: ## 9.1 Literature Review/References
L3053: Literature references are contained within the specific sections of the safety review.
L3054: As detailed in Section 8 of this review, NT 201 has been previously approved in a number of countries outside of the United States. A search of the literature for reports yielded the following:
L3055: - A search for the key word "Xeomin:" returned only 24 publications. These were summaries of safety and efficacy for various indications, and did not contain any adverse event case reports.
- A search for the key word "NT 201" returned only 9 publications. These were of the same nature as the publications for the key word "Xeomin" and there were no safety-related case reports.
L3056: ## 9.2 Labeling Recommendations
L3057: The sponsor's proposed labeling was insufficient in a number of respects, including lack of class labeling regarding spread of toxin effect that includes a Boxed Warning, inadequate discussion of overdose that is class labeling, and inadequate presentation of adverse events.
L3058: ## 9.3 Advisory Committee Meeting
L3059: There will be no advisory committee meeting regarding NT 201.
L3060: ## 9.4 FDA Meeting Minutes (February 2, 2009) with Pre-BLA Exposure Specifications
L3061: **Question 8:** Does the Division share the applicant's view that the proposed safety database is adequate to support an application for the indications cervical dystonia (b) (4)r
L3062: ## **Preliminary FDA Response:**
L3063: The briefing document suggests that there were extension periods for the pivotal efficacy trials in each indication. However, it is not clear how many subjects were in the extension period for each of these indications, or how many doses were administered to each subject and for how long the observation period was continued. In particular, your long term safety data (i.e. up to 1 year of exposure) must cover the full range of doses recommended for each indication (i.e. up to 400U per treatment). Please clarify the extent of data available by duration (e.g. up to 6 months, up to 12 months) and by dose (e.g. up to 200U, up to 300U, up to 400U).
L3064: Please provide analyses of the effects on blood glucose and bicarbonate by dose and duration in your NDA.
L3065: ## **Meeting Discussion:**
L3066: *The sponsor noted that at the time of the BLA filing they will only have single dose data, which they believe is sufficient because NT-201 is not the first in its class and botulinum toxins are as a class well studied. The Division noted that even though NT-201 is not the first in its class, it is clearly not identical to other botulinum toxin products, and safety related to multiple dosing, injection location, different doses and immunogenicity may not adequately be extrapolated from single-dose data. The Division stated that the lack of chronic use data in the BLA submission is a problem in the context of the typical requirements of six months of exposure to at least 300 patients and one year exposure to at least 100 patients; and further, (b) (4) that chronic use data from the ongoing openlabel studies can be included in the BLA. The sponsor agreed to (b) (4) include the data from the open-label extension of the spasticity study (MRZ-0410) that will provide data from 60 patients exposed for 1 year at 400 units, 30 patients exposed for 1 year at 300-400 units and 30 patients exposed for one year at 200-300 units. In addition, 85 patients with cervical dystonia will have been treated for one year at 240 units but the data will still be blinded at the filing, which the Division found acceptable as long as long term safety data from the indication with the highest dose and duration was included in the BLA. The Division noted that the BLA should be sufficient with respect to the full data package at the time of filing.*
L3067: *The sponsor agreed to provide the above requested analysis of the effects on blood glucose, but not on bicarbonate as this data was not collected.*

L3105: 1 Subject 003 experienced a concussion, which was mis-coded to cerebral disorder (and is shown as cerebral disorder in the summaries).
L3106: 2 Subject 444 experienced cervicocranial syndrome, which was mis-coded to cerebral ischemia (and is shown as cerebral ischemia in the summaries).
L3107: 3 In Study 9801, subjects received two treatments. Each dose of study medication was divided as follows: 1/3 of the total dose (in units) into the splenius capitis, and 2/3 into the sternocleidomastoid.
L3108: 4 Subject 2064: The events cardiomyopathy and cardiac failure congestive are not in the current clinical database because, although a report of these events has been received, they were not yet coded at the time of the cutoff for the summary tables.
L3109: 5 Subject 1197 had a colostomy, but the event was mis-coded to "preternatural anus" (and is shown as preternatural anus in the SAE summaries).
L3110: 6 In Studies 0520 (GFL) and 0527 (GFL), "treatment-naïve" is defined as not having received Botulinum toxin in the 12 months before study entry.
L3111: For sites of administration in each study, see ISS Table 1, Table 2, and Table 3.
L3112: No SAEs occurred in Studies 0703 (BEB), 9901 (HV), and 0709 (HV).
L3113: f/u: follow-up; MedDRA: Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities; OLEX: open-label extension (period); U: units; w/: with.

L3125: This 75-year-old male subject (white, weight 239 lb, height 70.5 in) was a non-smoker. His medical history was significant for cholecystectomy in 1986, haemorrhoid operation in 1986, right shoulder injury in 1996, prostate cancer from JUN-2000 until NOV-2000, surgical removal of prostate cancer in (b) (6) and skin cancer removed from right side of face on (b) (6) (b) (6). Concomitant diseases included hypothyroidism since 1976, blurred vision due to macular degeneration since 1998, seasonal allergies since 25-MAY-1999, gastrooesophageal reflux disease since 2001, corneal dystrophy since 26-MAR-2001, macular degeneration since 21-APR-2003, cataract since 19-JUL-2005, hypertension since February 2006, atrial fibrillation since 12-OCT-2006, and dry eyes since 28-NOV-2006. Concomitant medications were levothyroxine sodium 0.1µg/day [sic] for hypothyroidism, omeprazole 20 mg/days for gastrooesophageal reflux disease, cyproheptadine hydrochloride 1.2 mg/day for blepharospasm, Ocuvite vitamins (daily dose unknown) as dietary supplement, hydrochlorothiazide (daily dose unknown) and metoprolol succinate 100 mg/day for hypertension. Blepharospasm was first diagnosed in May 1990. Estimated duration of BEB was 16 years and five months. Before entering the study the subject had been treated for almost 16 years with 100 U Botox®. The last injection prior to study entry was on 13-JUN-2006 with Botox® 100 U. The subject signed informed consent on 12-OCT-2006 and received a total dose of 100 U of NT 201 on 24-OCT-2006. On 06-NOV-2006 the subject experienced dry mouth, which was rated as mild in intensity and not related to treatment by the investigator. This event resolved on 05-DEC-2006, no action was taken regarding the adverse event. The subject did not experience any other adverse events in the Main Period and continued in the Open Label Extension Period.

EFFICACY
14.	
Do there appear to be the requisite number of adequate and well-controlled studies in the application?
Pivotal Study #1 MRZ 60201-0433/1 Main Period: Prospective, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized, multi-center trial with open-label extension period to investigate efficacy and safety of NT 201 in the treatment of Blepharospasm
MRZ 0433/2 OLEX (Extension Period): open-label extension period to investigate continued efficacy and safety of NT 201 in the treatment of blepharospasm
X	
Single injection session of NT 201 compared to placebo. Subjects were previously treated successfully with Botox. N=109 in ITT (75 NT 201, 34 PBO) from 34 centers in US and Canada. Primary efficacy variable: change from baseline in the JRS Severity subscore at six weeks.
Open label extension period: investigate safety and efficacy of repeated injections of NT 201 in subjects with BEB over a 1 yr period = OLEX period.

L3199: Text Figure 1: Study Design (Main Period and OLEX Period)
MAIN PERIOD
(7 – 21 WEEKS)	OPEN-LABEL EXTENSION PERIOD
(48 – 69 WEEKS)
SCREENING
(1 WEEK)	PLACEBO-CONTROLLED
PERIOD (6-20 WEEKS)1)	OPEN-LABEL TREATMENT PERIOD
(max. 48 WEEKS +1 WEEK)	SAFETY PERIOD
(max. 20 WEEKS)
The timeline illustrates the study phases:
Screening Visit (Visit 1) marks the start of the Screening Period.
Baseline Visit (Visit 2) marks the start of the Placebo period.
Final Visit of Main Period (Visit 5) marks the end of the Placebo period and the start of the NT 201 period.
Repeated injections (max. 5, as required but at least 6 weeks in-between) occur during the NT 201 period.
Last injection (Week 48 at the latest, -1 week tolerance) marks the end of the NT 201 period.
Trial Termination Visit (max. 20 weeks after last injection) marks the end of the Safety Period.
Legend:
individual duration of placebo-controlled period per patient – see 9.1.3.1 for determination method
telephone contact Day 28-35
NT 201 as in the previous two injection sessions
control visits 6 weeks after each injection

L3204: Figure 11 is a box plot illustrating the Trial 0433/1 JRS-S scores at baseline (Visit 2) by arm. The Y-axis represents the JRS-S2 score, ranging from 1 to 4. The X-axis represents the ARM, comparing NT 201 and PCB groups. Both arms show similar distributions of scores, centered around 3.0 to 3.2. The NT 201 group has a slightly higher median score (around 3.2) compared to the PCB group (around 3.0). The interquartile ranges (IQR) and overall distributions overlap significantly, suggesting no substantial difference in baseline scores between the two arms.

The data points show a negative correlation, indicating that higher baseline JRS-S scores are associated with greater improvement (less negative change) at Visit 4.
L3208: Box plot showing the JRS-F Change from baseline to week 6 (Visit 4 - Visit 2) for two arms: NT 201 and PCB. The Y-axis is JRS-F Change from baseline, ranging from -4 to 2. The X-axis is ARM (NT 201 and PCB). For NT 201, the median change is slightly negative (around -0.5), with the box spanning approximately -2 to 0.5. For PCB, the median change is slightly positive (around 0.1), with the box spanning approximately -0.5 to 0.5. Both arms show a distribution centered near zero change.
L3209: Figure 16 is a box plot illustrating the change in JRS-S Patient Diary scores from baseline (BL) to Week 6 (Week 6 - BL) for two treatment arms: NT 201 and Placebo. The Y-axis represents the JRS Diary (Week 6 - BL) score, ranging from -5 to 3. The X-axis represents the Arm (NT 201 and Placebo).
For the NT 201 arm, the median change is slightly negative (around -0.5), with the interquartile range (IQR) spanning approximately -1.5 to 0.5. The overall distribution ranges from about -4 to 1.5.
For the Placebo arm, the median change is slightly positive (around 0.5), with the IQR spanning approximately -0.5 to 1.5. The overall distribution ranges from about -1.5 to 2.5.
Both arms show a distribution centered around zero, suggesting minimal overall change from baseline to Week 6.
L3210: Figure 17 is a Leverage Plot showing the relationship between the JRS Diary Baseline Score (X-axis) and the JRS Diary (Week 6 - BL) Leverage Residuals (Y-axis). The X-axis ranges from 0 to 4, and the Y-axis ranges from -4 to 3.
The plot displays several data points (residuals) and two fitted regression lines (solid and dashed). The residuals generally show a negative correlation with the baseline score, indicating that higher baseline scores are associated with lower residuals (worse outcome relative to prediction).
The statistical significance of the leverage is indicated by the text: Leverage, P=0.0338.

The NT 201 arm shows a median change near 0.0, with the box spanning approximately -0.5 to 0.5. The PCB arm shows a median change near 0.0, with the box spanning approximately -0.5 to 0.5. Both arms exhibit similar distributions of change in BSDI.
